#5179. Ripple's XRP Ledger Lending to Receive Repayment Fix

The XRP Ledger's lending protocol is in for a tidy-up of how loan repayments get rounded. In plain terms, the balance after each payment would match the schedule, the update has been discribed in the official Github pull request.

After an overpayment, TotalValueOutstanding is reset to the scheduled value of the new PeriodicPayment.

Under the LendingProtocolV1_2 amendment, each on-time LoanPay would bring TotalValueOutstanding down to the scheduled value with one payment fewer, capped at the rounded PeriodicPayment.

The first commit moves the scheduled-value calculation out of computeLoanProperties into a separate scheduledValue function, with no behavior change.

That way, LoanSet and LoanPay round it the same way. Zero-interest loans also need a floor on the principal part, because their value is rounded to nearest while the principal is rounded up. Tests run on Legacy, CashBasis and FixedPrecision vaults, with and without the amendment.

Today, each payment's value part is calculated on its own, so rounding can build up across payments. The last payment can then need one unit more than the rounded PeriodicPayment, and the value parts can alternate between payments.

With the change, a method called telescoping makes the value part of every on-time payment either the floor or the ceiling of PeriodicPayment, and at least one unit. The last payment becomes exactly the rounded PeriodicPayment.

There's little to no effect for users and investors today. The extra unit on the final payment changes little. Older loans that already carried one unit above their schedule simply keep it, so the last payment still needs the PeriodicPayment and a single unit, unless the borrower overpays first.

Small overpayments are mostly ignored; the transaction goes through successfully and charges no extra XRP whatsoever.

Activation date is yet to be determined: 80% of validators agreement is needed for two weeks in a row for the amendment to go through. No public vote for LendingProtocolV1_2 has been announced yet, so the timeline remains uncertain.

#5178. Higher wages don’t have to mean higher inflation. That could matter for Bitcoin

Preliminary Q3 productivity outpaced hourly compensation, offering a cost counterpoint while inflation keeps the Fed cautious.

US nonfarm businesses produced more per hour in the third quarter, allowing labor costs per unit of output to fall even as hourly compensation rose. For Bitcoin, that offers a potential offset to jobs-driven rate fears: if lower cost pressure helps cool inflation and expected interest rates, financing could become less expensive.

The Bureau of Labor Statistics' preliminary November 5 release showed nonfarm business productivity rising 3.6% as output increased 3.7% and hours worked rose 0.1%. Hourly compensation increased 2.9%, while unit labor costs fell 0.7%. All those quarterly changes are seasonally adjusted annualized rates.

Unit labor costs track compensation relative to output. BLS divides hourly compensation by productivity: when each hour produces more, businesses can pay more per hour without incurring higher labor costs for each unit they produce.

Q3 productivity grew faster than hourly compensation. The quarterly cost decline coexisted with a positive year-over-year cost reading.

Productivity can absorb higher pay; quarterly labor-cost relief does not settle the inflation outlook.

Preliminary Q3 nonfarm business estimates, published November 5, 2026. Quarterly figures are seasonally adjusted annualized rates; the 2.4% figure is year over year. This is not consumer-price inflation, October productivity or a Bitcoin response.

Plot reported Q3 annualized changes on one linear scale: 80 SVG units per percentage point on desktop and 44 on mobile. The 2.4% four-quarter change is shown separately without a comparable bar. No growth-rate subtraction or new calculation.

Preliminary Q3 nonfarm business estimates, published November 5, 2026. Quarterly figures are seasonally adjusted annualized rates; the 2.4% figure is year over year. This is not consumer-price inflation, October productivity or a Bitcoin response.

That complicates a reading of the November 6 employment report as an automatic inflation warning. October payrolls increased 165,000, above the prior 12 months' average monthly gain of 48,000. Average hourly earnings for private nonfarm employees rose 0.4% over the month and 3.1% over a year.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The reports cover different periods and measures. Third-quarter productivity does not establish how productive workers were in October, and its annualized growth rate cannot simply be subtracted from monthly earnings growth. Higher hiring and pay alone leave the question of labor costs per unit of output unanswered.

Nonfarm business unit labor costs remained 2.4% higher than a year earlier. The quarterly decline eases one source of cost pressure, while leaving both the durability of that improvement and its effect on consumer prices unsettled.

The Fed's latest decision illustrates the policy tension. On October 28, before either November labor release, the Federal Open Market Committee held its target range at 3.75% to 4.00% in a 10-2 vote. Lisa Cook and Beth Hammack preferred a quarter-point increase.

That statement described productivity growth as strong while calling inflation elevated. The two conditions already coexisted in the Fed's assessment, limiting the case for treating better productivity as an automatic signal of easier policy.

For Bitcoin, the financing channel depends on what happens to rate expectations. Expected short-term rates can influence longer-term rates, borrowing terms and the relative attractiveness of assets. A sustained decline in inflation pressure could ease a potential rates headwind for Bitcoin if it brings lower expected rates and funding costs.

Persistent inflation or upward labor-cost revisions would weaken the case for relief in Bitcoin prices.

#5176. Tornado Cash Whale Deposits $7 Million to Hyperliquid for Massive Bitcoin Long

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

An anonymous Tornado Cash whale sent $7 million to Hyperliquid to open a large long on Bitcoin this morning. According to Lookonchain and Onchain Lens, the wallet went in with aggressive 20x leverage with the plan to profit from Bitcoin crushing Ether.

Large holders usually run funds through mixers to move capital privately or to get ready to cash out. This case is unusual as the movement of these funds had just previously been tracked to a chain of addresses belonging to the hackers who drained the derivatives platform Drift Protocol of $290 million in April 2026.

Some blockchain researchers warn against jumping to conclusions about where the funds came from. There's still no direct evidence: Tornado Cash covers its tracks well, and the link between the wallet and specific hacks rests only on circumstantial matches in volume.

After starting as a brand-new address that withdrew 3,100 ETH from Tornado Cash, the unknown later swapped all of the Ether through CoW Swap on the spot, locking in $6.92 million in USDC. From there, the money went to the decentralized exchange Hyperliquid as collateral for future trades.

On the exchange, the whale didn't try to guess where the market was headed and just split the collateral in half, turned on 20x cross leverage and built a position worth about $26 million in total. The position consisted of $12.84 million at an entry price of $75,762.2 and an Ethereum short of 5,740 ETH worth $12.83 million.

So far, the bet is working. Bitcoin is holding near local highs, and the gain on the BTC long fully covers the drawdown from altcoin short. Add to this the earnings on funding, collecting a regular premium from other traders for holding the ETH short.

This is a textbook dominance pair trade, where the investor couldn't care less whether the industry as a whole goes up or down and the only thing that matters is the spread - the price gap between the two largest coins.

If Bitcoin rises faster than Ether, or if Ether falls harder than Bitcoin in a broad market crash, the trade profits.

The math is elegant, but 20x leverage is always a high risk. Should Ethereum rip higher while Bitcoin stays stuck in a range, the margin will be wiped out in seconds as Hyperliquid's cross-margin system will force-close the orders, and the investor will lose the entire $6.92 million. The liquidation price is set at $37,014.09 for BTC and $3,343.60 for ETH.

#5175. 5 Reasons to Fall in Love with La Quinta

Whether you’re visiting to play, explore, or simply chill, this desert paradise in California’s Coachella Valley has a little something for everyone.

Every day, from deciding whether or not to hit snooze to choosing to read a few more pages before shutting our eyes, our brains are a blur of choices—approximately 35,000 according to prevailing research. And frankly, it’s exhausting trying to make the right one every time. A vacation reboot would help… but travel planning requires even more decisions, which obviously defeats the purpose. And so the trip gets backburnered again.

What if there was a place that made choosing easy—because there’s no possible wrong answer? A land where you could decide to do all the things on the visitor’s bureau checklist, or none of them, and have an equally good time?

Enter La Quinta, California, a city of championship golf courses, towering palms, sprawling resorts with pools and spas, Old Hollywood lore, dramatic mountain backdrops, purple skies at dusk, and savory restaurants tucked into a calm corner of the Coachella Valley. It’s a place where you’re encouraged to do only what you love and what makes you feel most alive. Here in this sun-kissed slice of paradise, choose to follow the itinerary that is most Quinta-ssentially you.

There are 20 golf courses within the city limits, including the nine at the PGA West, a 2,000-acre luxury golf resort and community. Among those are the Mountain Course and the Stadium Course, which blend stunning natural beauty, manicured landscapes, challenging holes and expert designs by masters Pete Dye, Jack Nicklaus, and Greg Norman.

You can hit up the pickleball and tennis courts at Fritz Burns Park from dawn until well past dusk. The 12-acre green space also features a skate park, a children’s playground, a pool, and a dedicated area to play fetch with your pup. If you need to brush up on the rules of your chosen game, book some time with the pros at La Quinta Resort & Club, where you can rent equipment or pick up a match.

Whether you’re craving laid-back brunch bites, like the huckleberry hotcakes or double smashburgers at the farm-to-table eatery Beautiful Day, or a night on the town enjoying minty lamb chops and mai tais exploding with citrusy pucker at DSRT Club, La Quinta’s culinary scene does not disappoint.

Greet the day with Mexican delicacies like machaca or chilaquiles on the colorful porch at Casa Mendoza. If you have a hankering for south-of-the-border specialties later in the day, they also make a mean molcajete, seafood enchiladas, or tacos al pastor. Come afternoon, head to La Quinta Cliffhouse for the legendary nightly happy hour and stay for supper at sunset. There, you can put the date in date night by snacking on Medjools with bacon, blue cheese, coconut, and honey. (Pro tip: always order the gooey stone fruit however it’s served, whether as a shake, syrup or side dish—the Coachella Valley grows 95% of the dates in the U.S.) Close out the night with a slice of decadent Toll House Pie, a fixture at The Sandbar for four decades.

Those who believe it is just as important to nourish the mind as it is the body can take a walk down memory lane at the free La Quinta Museum, where the newly expanded history and culture exhibit details the people, technologies, and businesses that shaped the city, including the Cahuilla Indians, homesteaders, the railroad, and the celebrity magnet La Quinta Resort, which is celebrating its centennial this year. At the museum, visual learners can study the massive mural and a collection of photographs.

La Quinta itself is a patron of the arts. It’s hard to miss the more than 160 sculptures, murals, and decorated utility boxes installed through the Art In Public Places program. Twice a year, creatives from around the world descend on the civic center campus and turn it into a concert venue and an alfresco art gallery for a four-day festival. After admiring the artwork, head over to Old Town for locally made mementos. The charming retail district—known for its pleasing Spanish colonial architecture and vibrant sprays of bougainvillea—stocks gems at independent boutiques and galleries year-round and hosts the open-air Art on Main Street event on select Saturdays from November to April.

The area is a bonanza for outdoor activity. Bike along the base of the Santa Rosa Mountains using the paved, 2.7-mile-long Bear Creek Trail, or fix your attitude by gaining altitude on the miles of trails littered throughout the Santa Rosa and San Jacinto mountain ranges.

One popular route takes hikers a little more than six miles from the La Quinta Cove neighborhood to Lake Cahuilla, which is particularly peaceful at dawn or dusk. (The luckiest trekkers spy bighorn sheep.) Go straight to the lake to picnic, go horseback riding, or fish. Prolong your city escape by pitching a tent and camping at Cahuilla. Sunrise yoga and movie screenings are also offered lakeside.

While it’s clear there’s lots to do, know that, in the LQ, doing nothing is always on the table. Downtime happens to be a desert specialty, as there’s always a cold pool, a chaise lounge, and an umbrella drink calling your name. In fact, La Quinta Resort & Club boasts 42 pools across the 45-acre property, including a family-friendly splash zone and a more serene adults-only plunge with private cabanas and movie nights. (Don’t forget the 53 hot tubs for crisp Coachella nights.) Those who enjoy night swimming should post up at the never-closed pool at The Chateau at Lake La Quinta.

Finish the evening with stargazing and a glass of something sparkling in front of the fire pits. To really lean into relaxation, melt into a hot stone massage at Coral Spa or happily drift off while indulging in chromotherapy, a steam bath, or a dreamy scalp rub at Spa La Quinta. Or tap into an even deeper state of zen by joining a meditation or breathwork session or Sound Bath Sundays at Gather Yoga in Old Town. Plan your easiest trip ever today.

#5173. Zcash’s new security fund is 99.7% old money

A November 7 mainnet snapshot puts the historical seed at 99.7% of the reserve, with 1.12 ZEC accumulated under NU7’s fee rules.

Zcash’s new reserve for future block rewards is still overwhelmingly inherited value. About 99.7% of its balance in an early post-NU7 mainnet observation on November 7, 2026, came from previously unclaimed subsidy and fees, leaving just 1.12 ZEC accumulated under the new rules.

At 05:08:38 UTC, the mainnet API for Zakura’s NU7 monitoring dashboard reported a Network Sustainability Mechanism, or NSM, balance of 369.7 ZEC at block 3,547,068. Its status data showed NU7 active.

ZIP 237, the specification governing the reserve, sets the historical seed at 368.58445520 ZEC. Subtracting that seed leaves 1.11895065 ZEC of subsequent accumulation, or about 0.3% of the observed total.

An early mainnet snapshot separates the historical seed from new fee accumulation, then traces the fee path into future block subsidies.

At the observed height, 1.11895065 ZEC had accumulated beyond the seed. Future support depends on fee demand over time.

Zakura observation at 2026-11-07 05:08:38 UTC, height 3547068. Difference follows reported balance and specified accounting, not a separate block-fee audit. This opening snapshot does not forecast traffic or security adequacy. Reissuance is future at block 8939664.

Integer zatoshi: 36970340585 minus 36858445520 = 111895065. Divide by 100000000 for ZEC. Seed share = 36858445520 / 36970340585 = 99.69733829%; labels round to 99.70% and 0.30%. Bar widths use unrounded shares. Flow connectors are schematic, not quantitative.

Zakura observation at 2026-11-07 05:08:38 UTC, height 3547068. Difference follows reported balance and specified accounting, not a separate block-fee audit. This opening snapshot does not forecast traffic or security adequacy. Reissuance is future at block 8939664.

The distinction matters for Zcash’s future security funding. NU7 has established a way to recycle transaction fees into later block subsidies. Its opening accumulation provides an early measure of contributions; assessing future security support requires a much longer record of fee demand.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The historical seed consists of subsidy and transaction fees that miners left unclaimed in coinbase transactions before NU6. ZIP 237 brings that fixed amount into NSM accounting. It therefore gives the mechanism a positive starting balance before any new fee contribution arrives.

For the rules deployed at NU7, ZIP 237 registers transaction fees as the reserve’s only contribution mechanism. ZIP 259, the deployment specification, excludes ZIP 233’s separate removal mechanism and ZIP 234’s alternative issuance design.

There is also no reserve reissuance at the observed height. Taken together, those rules mean the excess over the historical seed represents accumulated fee contributions under the specified accounting. The 1.12 ZEC figure follows from the reported reserve balance and those accounting rules.

The NSM balance itself is public consensus accounting for value outside issued supply. Distribution follows the protocol’s future subsidy rules.

ZIP 235 directs 60% of each block’s aggregate transaction fees into the NSM. The contribution is rounded down once per block to whole zatoshi, Zcash’s smallest unit; miners receive the remainder, including the rounding difference.

Scheduled subsidy remains separate from this fee allocation. The mechanism temporarily removes fee value from circulation and returns it through future block subsidies.

The delay is substantial in block terms. ZIP 259 assigns mainnet activation to block 3,543,405, while reserve reissuance starts at block 8,939,664. ZIP 237 sets the additional NSM subsidy to zero before that later height. Reaching that block threshold determines when payouts begin.

Once reissuance starts, each block adds a small fraction of the preceding reserve balance to the scheduled subsidy, rounded up to a zatoshi. The existing halving schedule remains in place.

ZIP 237 illustrates the scale with 100,000 ZEC removed from circulation: that amount would initially contribute 0.01375 ZEC per block under the post-NU7 formula. This hypothetical illustrates the payout rule; the actual starting payout will depend on the reserve accumulated by then.

Funding streams also receive a share of future reissuance. ZIP 237 applies funding streams’ prescribed percentages to the combined scheduled and additional subsidy. The reserve mechanism and discretionary developer grants are different arrangements.

Before payouts begin, the fee-funded portion grows with cumulative fee spending. More time permits more contributions if fee-paying activity continues; elapsed time alone supplies no funding.

That makes the useful follow-up measure the amount added beyond the fixed historical seed over clearly dated intervals. A growing total can otherwise obscure how much of the reserve still comes from inherited value.

The opening snapshot cannot establish a sustainable annual funding rate, predict future usage or show whether eventual support will be sufficient for network security. Those questions depend on fee demand over time and the balance available when gradual reissuance begins.

#5170. US sanctions hit 41 companies accused of keeping Prince Group’s scam network running

Treasury says the network concealed ownership and sustained Prince Group operations, while US rules require covered assets to be blocked.

The US sanctioned four people and 41 entities on November 5, 2026, targeting a corporate network that Treasury says sustained Prince Group's operations after earlier sanctions. The designations subject covered assets to US blocking and reporting requirements, with restrictions also reaching companies that meet OFAC's ownership threshold.

The Office of Foreign Assets Control (OFAC) said the network helped Prince Group, which it has designated a transnational criminal organization, manage offshore structures, conceal beneficial ownership and maintain commercial activities. It links the group's affiliates to digital asset investment scams, romance scams and other online fraud, with many operations relying on trafficked and coerced workers in Southeast Asian compounds.

The action follows earlier Prince Group sanctions on October 14, 2025 and June 23, 2026. The newly sanctioned individuals are Qiu Yong, Zhao Mengyao, He Yujing and Visal Pang, according to the OFAC designation record.

One chain runs from Qiu Yong through Sun Speed Holdings Limited, registered in the British Virgin Islands, to Hong Kong money lender Premium Financial Limited. Treasury says Qiu ultimately owns the lender and controls other BVI and Hong Kong companies on Prince Group's behalf.

Zhao serves as Qiu's personal assistant and as secretary and co-director of several Hong Kong companies he ultimately controls, Treasury said.

Treasury traces Qiu Yong's ownership through an offshore company to a Hong Kong lender. Explore how US blocking obligations apply.

Designated property in the United States or US persons' possession or control is blocked and reportable to OFAC.

The standing 50 Percent Rule covers direct or indirect ownership, individually or in aggregate, by one or more blocked persons.

The named target and the ownership path both matter when assessing sanctions exposure.

The ownership diagram is a selected path within the larger network. Transaction prohibitions retain general or specific OFAC license and exemption qualifications and their stated US jurisdictional scope.

Map Treasury's reported ownership chain with directional connectors, without imputing share percentages to that chain. A separate selectable panel explains the standing 50 Percent Rule and covered-property obligations from the same announcement.

The ownership diagram is a selected path within the larger network. Transaction prohibitions retain general or specific OFAC license and exemption qualifications and their stated US jurisdictional scope.

Treasury also targeted He Yujing, the wife of Prince Group executive Chen Bo, and Pang. Treasury says Chen transferred ownership of a Tokyo mansion to He after the October 2025 sanctions action. It says Pang controls 11 Cambodian companies, several co-directed with Chen, whose own US designation came on June 23, 2026.

The November action also names companies linked to previously sanctioned Prince Group executives Dai An and Thet Li. Treasury says Singapore-based Greenbay Multi Media is wholly owned by Thet Li.

Designated persons' property and interests in property in the United States, or in US persons' possession or control, are blocked and must be reported to OFAC.

The standing ownership rule also blocks entities owned 50% or more, directly or indirectly and individually or in the aggregate, by one or more blocked persons. That makes the ownership chain relevant alongside the names on the sanctions list.

Unless a general or specific OFAC license authorizes a transaction, or an exemption applies, transactions involving designated or otherwise blocked property are generally prohibited for US persons or within or transiting the United States. Treasury says violations can bring civil or criminal penalties, with civil penalties possible on a strict liability basis.

For counterparties assessing exposure, OFAC's guidance calls for ownership due diligence on entities involved in transactions and account relationships.

#5168. Why Women Over 50 Should Add This Underrated Carry to Their Strength Routine

“Many people think core training means crunches or planks, but one of the most functional ways to strengthen the core is by learning to resist movement,” says personal trainer Alice Jordan.

Her go-to exercise for doing exactly that? The kettlebell suitcase carry.

“Holding a kettlebell in one hand and walking with good posture forces the deep abdominal muscles, obliques, and spinal stabilizers to work continuously to keep your body upright,” explains Jordan, who specializes in strength training during perimenopause.

“For women over 50, this is particularly valuable because it develops balance, posture and stability while also improving grip strength, which is an important marker of healthy aging,” notes Jordan.

Indeed, one landmark study published in The Lancet found that lower grip strength was consistently associated with a higher risk of early death—even after accounting for age, physical activity, smoking, body size, and other cardiovascular risk factors. Notably, the researchers reported that grip strength predicted somebody’s risk of dying better than systolic blood pressure.

The single-arm kettlebell suitcase carry, adds Jordan, “also translates directly into everyday activities such as carrying shopping, lifting luggage, or playing with grandchildren, making it a highly functional exercise.”

Below, Jordan explains how to do the exercise effectively and safely.

“Aim to include the exercise 2-3 times per week as part of a strength training program. It’s particularly effective when performed after your main strength exercises or as part of your core training,” adds Jordan.

As for how heavy a weight you should use for this exercise, Jordan recommends “choosing a kettlebell that feels challenging while still allowing you to maintain perfect posture throughout the walk.”

“You should be able to resist leaning to one side, keep your shoulders level and your core engaged from start to finish,” she adds.

“As a general guide, beginners might start with around 15-20 lb, while those with more strength training experience may use 20-30 lb or heavier, depending on their ability.”

#5166. Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

#5165. Thailand is cutting off Bitget. Users have until December 5 to prepare

The regulator asks customers to consider action on holdings before the block, while Bitget promises guidance on service access.

Bitget users in Thailand face a December 5, 2026 access block, with the country's Securities and Exchange Commission urging customers to consider action on assets held on the exchange before the restriction takes effect.

Thailand's SEC set out the deadline in its November 5, 2026 notice after filing a criminal complaint over alleged unlicensed exchange operations. It said the Ministry of Digital Economy and Society had been notified to block bitget.com, other Bitget domains and the exchange's application. The scheduled restriction concerns access from Thailand. SEC announcement

The SEC identified BG Limited as Bitget's platform provider. It alleged that the exchange charged trading fees and targeted Thai users during 2022-2025 through a Thai-language website, baht-value displays and Thai-language promotions.

The complaint also names Kritsana Kritsananuwat, whom the SEC accused of helping promote the exchange through Facebook and YouTube. The allegations remain subject to the criminal process. The regulator described investigation by inquiry officials, prosecution by public prosecutors and adjudication by the courts as subsequent steps.

The SEC has asked customers to consider their holdings before access is blocked. Its notice specifies no asset freeze, compulsory sale or separate withdrawal cutoff, leaving customers to decide how to handle assets on the platform ahead of the scheduled restriction.

The SEC set December 5, 2026 for access blocking and urged customers to consider action on platform assets beforehand.

The scheduled restriction covers website, domain and application access from Thailand.

Current account access and withdrawal availability for customers in Thailand have not been established.

#5162. Gold is still 25% below its peak even as demand reaches $188B in Q3

October's losses show why knowing who's buying gold is only part of understanding its price.

Central banks bought more gold in the third quarter of 2026 than consumers worldwide bought for jewelry.

According to the World Gold Council's latest demand report, central banks and other official institutions acquired an estimated net 321 metric tons, while jewelry consumption came to 313 tons.

In the same quarter a year earlier, jewelry buyers had consumed about 150 tons more than central banks acquired.

By September, the institutions buying gold for their reserves had overtaken the people buying it to wear, although roughly 63% of estimated official demand couldn't be traced through publicly reported country-level purchases.

The price barely kept pace with Bitcoin, which gained 42.7% during the quarter versus gold's 3.84%. Both attracted money through exchange-traded funds, so this wasn't a straightforward case of investors ditching one for the other. They bought both and got different results.

However, Bitcoin's lead came crashing down in October: gold fell 6.3%, while Bitcoin lost 7.3%. Governments kept accumulating bullion, and investors kept believing in scarce assets, but neither belief did much for that month's returns.

The buyers behind those beliefs, and their reasons for holding on, explain rather more than the safe-haven label does.

Global gold demand reached $188 billion in the third quarter, up 22% from a year earlier. That sounds like an impressive shopping spree until you weigh the purchases.

Buyers took home 1% less metal, at 1,369 tons, while the average quarterly price was 23% higher. Much of the extra spending covered the price increase rather than buying more gold.

That was especially apparent in jewelry, where consumption fell 17% to 313 tons, the weakest third quarter in the council's records since 2000. Buyers in China and India cut purchases or chose lighter and lower-carat pieces, adjusting what they bought to what they could afford.

Physical investment wasn't immune either: demand for bars and coins fell 19% to 268 tons. Paying more per ounce didn't make every buyer more eager to own one.

Central banks' net purchases rose 42% to 321 tons, while gold-backed ETFs added 211 tons, reversing the previous quarter's withdrawals.

Those purchases helped offset weaker demand from jewelry buyers and physical investors, giving the market support from buyers who wanted gold as a financial holding even as others balked at the cost.

The categories don't divide neatly into institutions buying and households retreating. Individuals own gold through ETFs, and institutions can own bars and coins.

But the numbers do show that demand weakened in traditional consumer and physical-investment markets, while official reserves and listed funds absorbed more metal. Gold was becoming a harder purchase to justify at the counter and an easier allocation to defend in a portfolio.

Of the estimated 321 tons in net purchases, only about 120 tons appeared in publicly reported country-level activity.

China disclosed additions of 63 tons and Poland 40 tons, making them the largest reported buyers, but the country disclosures leave approximately 201 tons of the council's estimate unaccounted for in the public figures.

Reporting delays and purchases governments don't disclose can explain activity missing from the country data, and the broader estimate may be revised. The gap alone also doesn't establish secret, coordinated purchases.

We have a much better estimate of the total than a complete account of who bought it, which limits how confidently we can interpret the buying.

Q3 2026 net official demand, using rounded inputs: 321 metric tons estimated in total, of which 120 were publicly reported. The remaining 201 are calculated by subtraction, not added to the total. Unreported activity can include delayed or undisclosed purchases; estimates may be revised.

Source: World Gold Council, Q3 2026 central-bank demand

Central banks acquired an estimated 675 tons in the first nine months of 2026, exceeding their purchases over the same period of 2025.

Their appetite is large enough to offset weakness elsewhere, but incomplete disclosures make it harder to attribute that appetite to particular governments or motives. The survey evidence offers more detail on the latter, with some important limits of its own.

Quarterly totals. Jewelry measures consumption; bars and coins, ETFs, and official institutions measure net demand. Negative ETF values mean net withdrawals. Official institutions include central banks. These selected channels do not exhaust total gold demand and do not separate household from institutional investors.

Source: World Gold Council / Metals Focus / Refinitiv GFMS, Q3 2026, Table 1

In the World Gold Council's 2026 central-bank survey, 74% of respondents expected the US dollar's share of global reserves to be lower in five years. That gives some basis for the argument that reserve managers want less dependence on the dollar.

The IMF's second-quarter foreign-exchange reserve data put the dollar's share at 56.7%, down from 57.18% in the preceding quarter. It remained the dominant reserve currency, with dollar holdings largely steady as holdings in other currencies expanded.

This series also excludes monetary gold, so it can't tell us how much gold replaced dollars.

Even the funding of gold purchases is less straightforward than the familiar sell-Treasuries-buy-bullion argument allows. Half the survey respondents said they'd finance new gold purchases through domestic programs using local currency, while 38% said they'd sell existing reserve assets.

Buying locally produced gold with local currency doesn't require a corresponding sale of US government debt, and the quarterly demand totals can't identify what financed every purchase.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Reserve managers can want more gold without wanting out of everything else. Bullion gives them an asset whose value doesn't depend on an issuer honoring a financial obligation, making it a way to reduce reliance on other governments' currencies and debt.

However, the bargain still includes a market price, and that price can fall enough to make the word “protection” feel rather generous over shorter periods.

Gold finished October at $3,913 an ounce, down 6.3% for the month and roughly 25% below its January peak, according to the World Gold Council's November market commentary.

Central bank demand hadn't spared holders that loss, because even a persistent buyer can't dictate the price everyone else trades at. Futures positions and the need to raise cash can overwhelm the reassurance of a government adding bullion to its reserves.

Global gold-backed ETFs attracted $2.7 billion in net inflows during October, adding 19 tons and taking holdings to a record 4,275 tons. Their combined assets under management still fell 6% to $542 billion, as the lower gold price more than offset the new money.

The funds held more metal and were worth less, an entirely possible outcome that gets lost when inflows are treated as a verdict on returns.

The broader point is consistent with a July 2026 IMF study, which found that gold's ability to hedge market risk depends on the shock and the economic conditions around it. Gold can help diversify a reserve portfolio over time without reliably sheltering it during extreme stress.

Someone reducing exposure to sovereign credit over several years and someone trying to avoid a loss this month may buy the same asset, but they're asking it to do different jobs.

Bitcoin gave investors a much better third quarter, gaining 42.7% to end September around $83,570, according to 21Shares' quarterly analysis.

US spot Bitcoin ETFs added about 79,800 BTC, worth about $6.2 billion, reversing roughly $4.8 billion in second-quarter withdrawals.

Their purchases were nearly twice the estimated 41,300 BTC newly mined over the period, giving the rally a measurable source of demand through funds available to professional and individual investors alike.

That comparison with new supply is striking, although newly mined coins are only one source of Bitcoin available to buy. Existing holders own a far larger stock, and some were reducing their positions while ETFs accumulated.

21Shares estimated that balances in the long-term-holder cohort fell by 248,000 BTC during the quarter. The figure isn't a direct count of exchange sales, since coins can leave the cohort through transfers or other classification effects, but it prevents ETF buying from being mistaken for a market in which everyone was adding to their holdings.

Global gold-backed funds attracted a record $31 billion in third-quarter inflows and added 211 tons of bullion, even as the metal's price gain fell well short of Bitcoin's.

The dollar totals cover different markets: the gold figure is global, while the Bitcoin figure covers US spot ETFs. Their respective market sizes and investor bases differ too, so treating the totals as a head-to-head contest would tell us little.

Investors added exposure to both assets, but their quarterly returns differed by nearly 39%, which offers little support for a simple account of Bitcoin rallying because money was leaving gold. It also limits what ETF data alone can explain.

Listed funds supplied demand in both markets, while trading elsewhere, including derivatives positions and borrowed money, helped determine how far each price moved.

October narrowed that enormous performance gap in an unwelcome way. Bitcoin fell 7.3% to about $77,500, while gold lost 6.3% over the same month.

Reported October 2026 returns in US dollars. Gold uses the LBMA PM benchmark through Oct. 30, the last business day; Bitcoin trades continuously. These are price returns, excluding fees and income, rather than ETF returns. Similar losses in one month do not establish a lasting correlation or a common cause.

Source: World Gold Council / Bloomberg / LBMA, October 2026, Table 1 · 21Shares, November 2026 Bitcoin outlook

Gold ETFs continued to attract net investment globally, led by Europe and Asia, while North American funds recorded modest withdrawals. On Nov. 4, Reuters reported that the largest Bitcoin-backed funds tracked by LSEG/Lipper lost $215 million in October.

While the totals don't establish that investors moved money from Bitcoin to gold, they show that net buying persisted across global bullion funds while the tracked Bitcoin funds lost money to withdrawals.

The World Gold Council pointed to reductions in speculative positions, revised expectations for Federal Reserve policy, and rotation between assets as possible contributors to gold's decline.

Bitcoin contended with weaker tech stocks, tighter credit conditions, the Binance hack, and the US midterm elections. Different sellers had different reasons to act, even though holders of both assets ended the month with losses.

Scarcity offers little defense against someone who needs cash or wants less exposure to volatile markets. Central-bank purchases can absorb gold without guaranteeing its price, just as Bitcoin's supply limit can constrain issuance without guaranteeing buyers at the price its holders would prefer.

October put both propositions on display, even while some investors continued to add bullion to their portfolios.

The sovereign comparison needs a careful approach, because a government owning Bitcoin doesn't necessarily mean it has entered the market to buy it.

The US established a Strategic Bitcoin Reserve in March 2025, formally giving Bitcoin a place among assets the federal government can retain and manage.

However, the executive order creating the reserve directed that it be funded primarily with Bitcoin already held through criminal or civil asset forfeitures.

Officials were also authorized to devise budget-neutral ways to acquire additional Bitcoin without incremental taxpayer costs. That permission and the decision to retain existing coins have policy importance, but neither establishes a recurring purchase program comparable with central banks adding gold to their reserves.

That's why putting seized Bitcoin into a reserve can happen without buying a single additional coin on the market.

The World Gold Council's quarterly figures estimate net additions to official gold holdings. The US reserve order governs, in large part, what happens to assets the government already owns.

Gold already has an established role in central bank balance sheets and reserve management. Bitcoin's newer sovereign role could evolve, but ETF access, government recognition, and official purchases each affect demand through a different route.

Investors can't assume progress on one will produce the buying associated with another, however attractive the reserve announcement sounds.

Interest-rate expectations and access to cash will continue to test both markets. Stronger US employment or inflation readings can encourage expectations of tighter Federal Reserve policy, increasing the appeal of interest-bearing assets relative to gold and potentially making financial conditions less accommodating for Bitcoin.

Weaker readings can support expectations of easier policy, although a deterioration severe enough to prompt fears of an economic slowdown can also leave investors wanting cash.

Neither the gold-demand report nor Bitcoin's ETF inflows can settle how investors will react to the next release. They can tell us more about who has been buying and how readily those buyers might leave.

Central banks can accumulate gold over several years, while ETF investors can cut exposure much faster. Consumers can buy lighter jewelry, and traders using borrowed money can face demands to reduce positions precisely when they'd prefer to wait.

That mix helps explain why persistent belief in an asset can coexist with a sharp fall in its price.

The reserve manager diversifying away from another government's obligations may have little reason to sell bullion during a correction, while an investor facing losses elsewhere may sell the same metal because it's readily tradable.

Bitcoin's fixed supply doesn't remove that need for cash, and official recognition doesn't give its holders the resources or mandate to ride out every downturn.

Central banks overtaking jewelry buyers shows how gold demand is being redistributed, and October's losses show the limits of what those purchases can promise other holders.

Someone buying for protection needs the asset's behavior to fit the risk they're trying to reduce and the period they can afford to hold it.

Gold can serve a reserve manager well over years and disappoint an investor over weeks, while Bitcoin can deliver a spectacular quarter without becoming a dependable place to wait out the next sell-off.

#5161. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

#5156. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#5154. Argentine Bitcoiners Debut “The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto” Artwork in Downtown Buenos Aires

“The Genesis of Satoshi Nakamoto,” a 2.4-meter, multi-dimensional cube, was presented on October 29 at Polo Dot as part of the Open Fest of LABITCONF 2026. Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the Premio B·Arte, the work combines art, technology, and education in a journey through Bitcoin’s fundamental principles. The inauguration featured guided remarks by Rodolfo Andragnes and a musical performance by Mayumaná, according to an announcement by the organization.

Buenos Aires now has a monument dedicated to Satoshi Nakamoto, the person—or group of people—behind the creation of Bitcoin, whose identity remains unknown.

Driven by Bitcoin.ar through the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte, the monument was conceived not only as a tribute to Satoshi, but also as an artistic and educational experience. Through its five faces—four lateral and one upper—it proposes a journey to approach different concepts that explain Bitcoin’s origin, functioning, and philosophy.

During the inauguration, Rodolfo Andragnes — co-founder of Bitcoin.ar and LABITCONF — accompanied the public with words and explanations about the details of the work, guiding the reading of its various elements and the meaning behind each representation. The presentation was accompanied by a musical show by Mayumaná, which added rhythm and sound to the opening of this new space dedicated to Bitcoin’s history.

Each of the monument’s faces offers a gateway to a different aspect of Bitcoin. The figure of Satoshi Nakamoto, for example, represents the anonymous character of its creator and the collective dimension of a technology that, once set in motion, continued to grow thanks to thousands of people distributed around the world. The reflections and multiplied geometric forms also evoke the structure of a network that replicates and expands.

Another of its faces addresses Bitcoin’s programmed scarcity through a cube that successively divides into halves in a Fibonacci sequence. The representation refers to “the halving”, the mechanism that approximately every four years halves the reward miners receive for validating blocks and that regulates the issuance of new bitcoins. Starting at 50 bitcoins per block, the total reward gets cut in half every four years for roughly 130 years, until 21 million bitcoins have been emitted and the block reward becomes zero. This supply limit is iconic to the cryptocurrency and key to its qualities as hard money.

The work also incorporates the first words of Bitcoin’s foundational document, the white paper published by Satoshi Nakamoto in October 31st,  2008. There, the possibility of making electronic payments directly between people, without needing to go through a financial institution, is proposed. That idea constitutes a foundational principle of the technology.

Another face reproduces information from the genesis block, the first block of the Bitcoin chain, created on January 3, 2009. Among its elements appears the reference to the headline from The Times newspaper that Satoshi incorporated into that block, a historical mark that places Bitcoin’s birth in its context and anchors it to history and time.

Thus, the monument proposes something more than contemplating a sculpture: it invites one to walk around it, observe it from different angles, and discover the concepts behind each of its parts; “The intention is to bring Bitcoin closer to those who are already part of its community, but also to awaken the curiosity of those who do not yet know its history.”

The monument was created by Ricardo Alonso, engineer, and Celeste Difabio Videla, architect, a couple of artists from Bariloche who won the fifth edition of the Premio B·Arte. The work measures 2.40 meters in width, length, and height, and is made of stainless steel and glass.

Its construction involved teams and collaborators from different localities across the country, in a process that combined different trades, materials, and techniques. Aída Pippo, visual artist, researcher of new technologies applied to art, and member of the Board of Directors of Bitcoin.ar, led the project and coordinated the different stages of production and assembly.

“It gives me great pride to feel that this is something we can leave in our city and in our country,” Andragnes expressed during the presentation, highlighting the value of a collective initiative that seeks to leave a cultural and educational footprint.

For their part, the creators noted that they sought for the work to function as a meeting point for the community and, at the same time, as a gateway for those who do not yet know Bitcoin. “You don’t always have the possibility of leaving something for posterity,” they shared when speaking of the experience of seeing the monument finished and installed in Buenos Aires.

With this inauguration, Bitcoin.ar seeks for the monument to become a permanent resource for the city: a space that schools, universities, families, and visitors can approach to learn Bitcoin’s history, discover its principles, and open new conversations about the future of money and technology. The intention is that what is presented today as an artistic work can continue to grow through educational activities and new experiences, and remain in Buenos Aires as a legacy for future generations.

The project was financed by the Fundación Bitcoin Iberoamérica and represents a new way of bringing Bitcoin’s history and principles to public and cultural spaces, beyond screens and specialized environments.

#5152. Bitcoin erases post-election gains as Wall Street selloff triggers over $1 billion in crypto liquidations

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The selloff triggered a wave of forced liquidations across crypto derivatives markets, as total liquidations over the past 24 hours reached $1.09 billion, affecting 211,000 traders.

Bitcoin, Ethereum, and other cryptocurrencies erased their post-election gains on Thursday as a sharp selloff in U.S. technology and AI stocks spilled into cryptocurrency markets, triggering more than $1 billion in leveraged position liquidations over the past 24 hours.

Bitcoin (BTC) fell 6% to $75,700, after touching an intraday low of $75,568, while ether (ETH) dropped more than 7% to $2,247. The declines reversed Wednesday's rally, when bitcoin climbed back above $80,000 following the U.S. midterm elections and signs that the Binance hack had not caused broader market disruption.

Midterm election results appeared to generate short-term optimism, but analysts say they haven't resolved macroeconomic uncertainties.

As of 2 p.m. ET, the Dow Jones Industrial Average and S&P 500 were down about 3% for the day, while the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite had dropped 4.7%. The Cboe Volatility Index, or VIX, surged approximately 60%, reflecting heightened investor anxiety.

AI and bitcoin-related mining stocks were among the hardest hit, with Bitdeer and Keel Infrastructure each falling about 20%. American Bitcoin dropped 17% despite announcing a quarterly profit of $126 million. Crypto bellwethers Strategy and Coinbase were each down about 10%.

Of note, the Official Trump (TRUMP) memecoin hit an all-time low after falling more than 20% over the past two days.

The selloff also triggered a wave of forced liquidations across crypto derivatives markets. About $879.92 million in positions were liquidated over a four-hour period, including $818.90 million in longs and $61.02 million in shorts, according to CoinGlass. Total liquidations over the past 24 hours reached nearly $1.1 billion, affecting nearly 211,000 traders.

It should be noted that liquidation figures are typically based on publicly available data, which may significantly understate the extent of market liquidations.

The reversal suggests Wednesday's rebound may have been driven more by temporary optimism than a meaningful improvement in the market's underlying conditions, according to Wintermute.

The trading firm said the rally reflected a combination of relief following the Binance hack, enthusiasm surrounding the election results, optimism about a potential slowdown in AI spending and falling oil prices. However, those factors proved short-lived as broader macroeconomic pressures persisted.

"It's probably a combination of all of them in some way. What matters is that all of them seemed to be so short-lived that it actually didn't really matter," said Jasper De Maere, an OTC trader at Wintermute. "Trump is still the president for two years, the Middle East is still escalating, inflation is elevated into a strong U.S. economy."

Beyond the immediate market pressures, the election results may also complicate the outlook for crypto regulation, potentially tempering some of the optimism that helped drive Wednesday's rebound.

Paul Howard, senior director at Wincent, said the Democrats' strengthened position in Congress could make bipartisan cooperation necessary for meaningful progress on crypto legislation over the next two years. With the Clarity Act's prospects uncertain, Howard expects the existing regulatory framework to largely remain intact until the next election cycle.

Ryan Kirkley, CEO of Global Settlement Network, said the election could delay crypto legislation but wouldn't fundamentally change longer-term institutional demand for digital assets.

"Investors are still focused on liquidity, interest rates and the broader economy, and those factors will continue to influence the market regardless of which party controls Congress," Kirkley said.

If the weakness continues, Wintermute identified $73,000 and $68,000 as the next levels to watch for bitcoin. The firm said it would need to see bitcoin convincingly reclaim and hold $80,000 before turning more positive.

#5150. Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas has sued Kalshi, accusing the prediction market of offering an illegal, unregulated gambling platform to the state's residents, Attorney General Tim Griffin said on Wednesday.

The lawsuit, filed against KalshiEX LLC and Kalshi Inc., alleges multiple violations of the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act. The state is seeking restitution, penalties, taxes and an injunction.

Today I filed a lawsuit against @Kalshi for violating the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act by operating an unregulated gambling platform.

Kalshi has built a global business that it calls a prediction market, allowing consumers to wager money on the outcome of just about… pic.twitter.com/ePycNKXzy6

— Attorney General Tim Griffin (@AGTimGriffin) November 4, 2026

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said. "And gambling on an unregulated platform is squarely prohibited by Arkansas law."

Instead of obtaining licenses and paying the taxes required of gambling operators, Kalshi opened its platform to Arkansas residents as young as 18 and encouraged them to bet on sporting and entertainment events, Griffin alleged. "Arkansas law is concerned with substance, not semantics," he said.

Griffin pointed to Kalshi's own responsible-gambling tools, including self-exclusion, deposit limits and trading breaks, along with its partnership with the National Council on Problem Gambling, as evidence that the company knows its platform carries gambling-style risks. "Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," he said.

Kalshi, which is regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, has argued in other cases that its event contracts fall under exclusive federal jurisdiction and are not subject to state gambling laws.

Arkansas is the latest of more than a dozen states to take on prediction markets. Among the most significant cases:

Other states have also moved against prediction markets. Arizona filed criminal charges against Kalshi in March, a Washington court temporarily blocked its sports contracts in July, and Massachusetts' case is now before the state's highest court after a lower court barred Kalshi's sports contracts in January.

The CFTC has sued at least 10 states to defend what it says is its exclusive authority over prediction markets, and President Donald Trump has backed the agency, calling several state officials who challenged the platforms "scum."

Federal appeals courts are split. The Third Circuit ruled in April that New Jersey cannot regulate Kalshi's sports contracts, while the Sixth and Ninth Circuits sided with Ohio, Tennessee and Nevada. New Jersey has asked the Supreme Court to settle the question, backed by 39 other states and the District of Columbia, as well as the NFL. Kalshi's response to the petition is due November 9, and the Supreme Court is not expected to decide whether to take up the case until December at the earliest, ESPN reported.

#5148. South Korean Crypto Exchange Gopax Suspends Withdrawals and Trading After Hacking Threat

South Korea's Gopax halted standard withdrawals at 2:55 p.m. and trading at 3:53 p.m. Friday after a hacking-threat email, and is transferring all held assets to cold wallets with no loss reported so far.

South Korean cryptocurrency exchange Gopax suspended withdrawals and trading on Friday after receiving a hacking-threat email, and said it is moving all held virtual assets into offline cold wallets as a preemptive step to protect customer funds.

Gopax halted standard deposit and withdrawal services at 2:55 p.m. local time and stopped trading at 3:53 p.m., Yonhap News Agency reported, citing the exchange. Direct withdrawals through the website and app are unavailable. Customers who want coins out must apply through the customer support center, where requests are processed manually after internal verification.

“To ensure the safe custody of user assets, we plan to transfer all virtual assets to cold wallets for storage,” Gopax said in remarks carried by Seoul Economic Daily. “Direct withdrawals through the Gopax website or app are temporarily unavailable, and customers wishing to withdraw funds should apply through the Gopax customer support center.”

No actual loss of assets has been reported. The company has not set a date to reopen trading or standard withdrawals.

With trading offline, all open orders were canceled, Chosun Biz reported. Deposits were initially paused with withdrawals; later Korean coverage said deposits had been restored while web and app withdrawals stayed shut. Manual withdrawals through support will take longer than usual.

A cold wallet is a storage setup kept offline, cut off from the internet that hot wallets use to process customer sends. Gopax said the shift of remaining hot-wallet balances into cold storage is meant to shrink the surface available to anyone who can reach online systems.

A Gopax official told Yonhap the email came from a party claiming it could hack the exchange, but that the threat itself had not been verified. “We ask for your understanding, as this measure is intended to prevent financial incidents and protect user assets,” the company said.

The freeze lands in a week when exchange security is already under a bright light. On Oct. 29, attackers drained about $6.34 billion from Binance hot wallets in what Binance later described as a months-long campaign that began with a fake job interview. The FBI has tied that theft to North Korea; Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it is covering customer losses from its own funds.

Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin froze deposits and withdrawals earlier this week after its own security incident, with preliminary estimates of suspicious transfers near $40 million. Gopax’s case is different on the facts disclosed so far: the exchange is describing a threat email and a precautionary lockdown, not a confirmed drain.

Gopax itself has been dealing with account trouble. From early September, abnormal access hit some user accounts, leaking personal information on seven customers and two current or former employees, with further leakage confirmed last month, Yonhap’s Korean dispatch said. The company has treated that breach as separate from Friday’s threat email; any link has not been confirmed.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission has also been pressing domestic platforms after a broader wave of cybersecurity incidents across financial firms. Korean exchanges are already required to keep at least 80% of customer crypto in cold storage. Friday’s move pushes Gopax further offline while it checks whether the threat is real.

For bitcoin holders on Gopax, the practical effect is a queue: coins are being parked offline while support staff approve outbound sends one by one. That is slower than an app button, and it is the trade the exchange says it is making to keep balances intact.

The test for Gopax now is whether it can finish the cold-wallet transfer, clear the manual withdrawal queue without a loss, and say when trading comes back — or whether the unverified threat turns into the incident it was trying to get ahead of.

Overall, our testers appreciated the lightweight and airy feel of this material and that it was absorbent, rather than water-resistant. And while some liked the circular shape of this blanket, others would’ve preferred if the blanket were square- or rectangular-shaped like the other options on this list.

For on-the-go intimacy, your best bet is this travel-sized reversible blanket with a waterproof core. As with the Dame Thro, the cotton waffle weave is absorbent, not water-resistant, so liquids won’t pool on top of the blanket. And while it’s not entirely made of natural fibers (it’s 57 percent polyester), our testers liked how soft the material felt. They also liked the vibrant color combination of lilac and orange. Plus, it folds flat into a storage bag, so it’s easy to stash in your backseat for car sex.

The only potential downside is the price. It’s slightly more expensive than other sex blankets—both similar size and larger.

#5146. Hyperliquid Strategies files to become CFTC-registered FCM, posts $13.3 million in quaterly revenue

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The filing would let the HYPE treasury firm carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 perpetual futures markets, pending regulatory approval.

Nasdaq-listed Hyperliquid Strategies (PURR) reported $13.3 million in revenue for the quarter ended Sept. 30, exceeding analyst estimates of $10.6 million to $12 million, depending on the data provider.

The company posted $620.6 million in net income, largely driven by $803 million in unrealized gains on its Hyperliquid (HYPE) token holdings, partially offset by a $172.3 million income tax provision.

Perhaps most notably, Hyperliquid Strategies filed with the CFTC to become a futures commission merchant (FCM), which would allow the firm to carry U.S. client accounts for HIP-3 markets.

"Hyperliquid recently announced its intention to add additional deployer functionalities under the name HIP-3," the firm said in a release. "These optional additional features would allow deployers to build and launch permissioned markets on Hyperliquid's onchain infrastructure. Independent operators would run their own market in a compliant manner, while the chain underneath would stay a neutral infrastructure layer."

The FCM, which is subject to regulatory approval, would allow Hyperliquid Strategies to "play a role" in bringing perpetual futures to U.S. customers within a regulated framework.

"There can be no assurance as to the timing or outcome of the application or regulatory approval of permissioned HIP-3 markets," the firm said.

This comes as Hyperliquid's underlying infrastructure moves closer to the U.S. market. NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange agreed last week to partner with TradeXYZ to bring Hyperliquid-based perp futures markets to U.S. traders. ICE would operate the exchange and clearing infrastructure, with TradeXYZ designing the markets.

Earlier this year, President Donald Trump said his administration was working to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S. "in a fully compliant and legal fashion."

#5144. Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

A Canadian man has been extradited to the United States to face charges that he helped hack into university computers to steal login credentials and secretly mine cryptocurrency, the Justice Department said on Tuesday.

A federal grand jury in the Western District of New York indicted Ryan James Roach, 35, of Saskatchewan, and Mathiew James Stubbings, 41, of Ontario, on charges of conspiracy to obtain information from and damage a protected computer, obtaining information from a protected computer, and intentionally damaging a protected computer. The charges carry a maximum sentence of 10 years in prison and a $250,000 fine.

Canadian man extradited to the U.S. for his role in computer hacking scheme targeting universities... @FBIBuffalo @USDOJ_Intl https://t.co/vY8IacXdZZ pic.twitter.com/NsSZ5BIkIf

Between June 2016 and December 2019, the pair targeted U.S. educational institutions with supercomputers, researching security weaknesses in their networks before breaking in, according to the indictment. Once inside, they allegedly deployed malware, stole login credentials and forced the victims' computers to mine cryptocurrency for their own benefit.

Victims included institutions in New York, Tennessee, North Carolina and Massachusetts, among them a university in the Western District of New York, prosecutors said. The pair allegedly used virtual private servers in several countries, registered under false names, to send malware and mining software to victim computers and store stolen credentials, and routed attacks through computers compromised at other victims to hide their identities.

Roach was extradited from Canada on October 28 and arraigned in Rochester, New York, where he was held pending a detention hearing. Extradition proceedings against Stubbings are continuing in Canada.

The scheme is a form of cryptojacking, in which attackers use other people's computers to mine crypto while the victims bear the electricity costs and slower performance. The Justice Department did not say which cryptocurrency the pair allegedly mined.

But hackers looking to profit from stolen computing power may be turning elsewhere. In a report published Monday, Google's Threat Intelligence Group said it expects attackers to "increasingly prioritize LLMJacking over traditional resource theft like cryptojacking," hijacking companies' access to AI models and passing on the bills.

Unlike the gradual drain of cryptojacking, LLMjacking can run up huge cloud charges within hours, Google said. In incidents investigated by its Mandiant unit this year, fraudulent charges topped $100,000 in a single day, and some organizations racked up $1 million before access was cut off. Attackers are reselling stolen access through underground services that Google calls "LLM Mixers."
