#5142. Bitcoin Price Holds Near $75,000 After Hot October Jobs Report

U.S. employers added 165,000 jobs in October, nearly three times the 59,000 economists expected, the Labor Department said Friday, and the bitcoin price briefly pushed above $76,100 before settling near $75,500, Bitcoin Magazine Pro data shows.

The unemployment rate edged down to 4.1% from 4.2%, beating the Dow Jones forecast that called for it to hold. Average hourly earnings rose 0.4% on the month and 3.1% over the year, both ahead of estimates, CNBC reported.

Prior months were revised higher as well. August payrolls moved to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, and September rose to 60,000 after a 31,000 upgrade, leaving the two-month total 84,000 stronger than first reported.

The print lands on a market still digesting Thursday’s Nasdaq-led rout. The bitcoin price had held near $76,000 into that close, dipped toward $75,200 late in the session, then recovered into the mid-$75,000s overnight before the 8:30 a.m. ET release. Spot bitcoin changed hands around $75,800 after the data, with a market cap of about $1.52 trillion and roughly $48 billion in trading volume over the past day, Yahoo Finance data indicated.

Bitcoin price’s first move was a quick spike above $76,100, then a slide toward $75,600 before it settled back near $75,800. The range on the day has run from about $75,240 to $76,280, leaving the market little changed from its pre-release level even as rate futures repriced.

That muted finish fits the pattern after big macro prints. Most of the damage to the bitcoin price this week came from equity risk-off, not from the labor market itself, and a hot payrolls number mainly reinforces the path the Fed was already pricing.

CME FedWatch odds put roughly a 79% chance of a 25-basis-point hike at the Dec. 9 meeting after the release, up from about 70% into the open. The next employment report is due Dec. 4, five days before that decision, and the signal points the same way the last Fed hold did: stronger hiring and firmer wages leave less room for another pause.

Thursday’s session still sets the floor under the bitcoin price. The Nasdaq Composite fell about 5.5% and dropped into correction territory, more than 10% below its recent high, while the S&P 500 lost about 4.4% and the Dow shed roughly 1,800 points, or 3.4%, CNBC reported.

The Magnificent Seven erased more than $1 trillion in market value. Nvidia alone lost more than 9%, wiping out roughly $440 billion in capitalization, as investors priced in a slower AI data-center buildout after the Democratic midterm sweep.

Bitcoin has traded with that AI-heavy complex for most of the year, so a correction in the Nasdaq has pulled the bitcoin price lower even when on-chain flows looked steady. Miners that leased power and floor space to AI tenants are caught in both trades.

Fund flows followed the risk-off tape. U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs posted $430.2 million in net outflows on Thursday, led by about $200 million from Fidelity’s FBTC and roughly $105 million from BlackRock’s IBIT, bringing week-to-date redemptions through Thursday to about $870 million.

Leverage kept the bitcoin price soft into the overnight. More than $1 billion in crypto positions were liquidated over 24 hours, mostly long bets built during Wednesday’s short-squeeze bounce that lifted bitcoin near $80,000.

Professional and business services led October hiring with 70,000 jobs, health care added 42,000, and transportation and warehousing gained 25,000. Manufacturing rose 19,000. The breadth undercuts the recent pattern in which health care carried most of the gains.

Policy risk has not eased either. House Democrats are already drafting their own market structure bill ahead of taking the committees in January, a backdrop that has hung over crypto equities since the midterm results first hit the bitcoin price.

Traders are watching whether the bitcoin price can defend the mid-$75,000 zone that held on election night and several times since August. A clean break would leave September’s low back in sight after a week that erased most of the post-election squeeze, now with a hotter labor market and firmer hike odds stacked on top.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $75,500.

#5139. Ark Invest buys more Coinbase as shares plunge 10% in wider market rout

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Cathie Wood's Ark Invest bought $2.44 million worth of Coinbase, while selling $2.27 million in Bullish and $2.28 million in Bitmine shares.

Cathie Wood-led investment firm Ark Invest bought additional shares of Coinbase on Thursday but trimmed its holdings in Bullish and Bitmine amid a sharp sell-off in U.S. technology stocks.

According to its latest trading disclosure, Ark bought 17,806 shares of Coinbase, worth $2.44 million, through its Ark Innovation ETF on Thursday. The firm also sold 109,522 shares of Bullish, worth $2.27 million, and 119,841 Bitmine shares, worth $2.28 million, via its ETFs.

Ark's move came as major U.S. technology stocks plummeted in a market rout that erased post-election gains. The tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite index closed down 5.52%, posting its worst day since April 2025, according to CNN. The S&P 500 lost 4.36%, and the Dow Jones Industrial Average dropped 3.44%.

Amid the sell-off, Ark purchased 252,592 Nvidia shares, worth $45.9 million, and 48,984 AMD shares, worth $19.8 million. The firm also added 393,774 SpaceX shares, worth $48.7 million, according to its trading disclosure.

The sell-off extended into Asian markets on Friday morning. South Korea's Kospi was down 3.6% around noon after plunging nearly 9% earlier in the day, triggering a brief trading halt. Japan's Nikkei 225 dropped 5.3% during the morning session.

Meanwhile, bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) traded down 5.8% over the past 24 hours at $75,908 as of 11:20 p.m. ET Thursday, after falling to around $75,350 in a local low earlier in the day. Ethereum Ethereum (ETH) dropped 8.2% to $2,234, according to The Block's crypto price page.

#5138. Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance has removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus, a third-party crypto trading platform, after SignalPlus reported a "potential security issue," the exchange said on Thursday.

"Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe," the exchange said, describing the move as an "immediate precautionary measure."

Following a potential security issue reported by third-party platform SignalPlus, we have proactively removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus as an immediate precautionary measure.

Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe.

If you used SignalPlus, please… pic.twitter.com/0HYYkTAW2p

API keys let third-party apps and trading tools connect to a user's exchange account and place trades on their behalf. Removing them cuts off that connection, meaning any automated strategies running on Binance through SignalPlus will stop until users set up new keys.

Binance urged SignalPlus users to log into their accounts directly to review their positions and open orders. It recommended that they wait until SignalPlus confirms the issue is fully resolved before reconnecting, and that they always enable IP whitelisting, which restricts an API key to approved internet addresses.

The exchange also called on all third-party platforms integrated with Binance to review their security controls, watch for unusual activity and contact it immediately if they spot any concerns.

SignalPlus, a Hong Kong-based platform that provides trading tools for crypto options and derivatives, connects to several exchanges, including Binance.

The incident comes a week after Binance was hit by the largest hack in crypto history, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from its hot wallets on October 29. The FBI has attributed the theft to North Korean hackers it calls TraderTraitor, a claim Pyongyang has denied.

Binance has said the attack began when a fake recruiter persuaded one of its engineers to download a malware-laced coding assignment, and that the attackers then used stolen credentials to reach an internal wallet signing service.

Binance said on Wednesday that it has replenished about 98% of the stolen assets from its own funds, and is still buying the rest, mostly less liquid tokens, through market and over-the-counter channels. It has also taken a new proof-of-reserves snapshot covering 44 tokens, up from 10, which users will be able to verify once its zero-knowledge proof is generated in about 50 to 60 hours.

The exchange is still restoring services. On Thursday, it began reopening withdrawals for more assets in phases, prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. Deposits remain open.

#5136. Binance Hackers Swap About $50 Million of Stolen XRP Mostly Into Bitcoin, Elliptic Says

Hackers tied to the record Binance theft have begun converting a large slice of the stolen XRP into bitcoin, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said in an update dated through Friday, with about 42 million XRP — roughly $50 million — already swapped, mostly through THORChain.

Converted bitcoin from other stolen assets nearly doubled overnight to about 1,650 BTC, worth some $124 million, Elliptic wrote. Most of that jump came from the XRP route. About 192 million XRP, roughly $226 million, remained unswapped in addresses linked to the Binance theft.

The XRP was part of the Oct. 29 drain that Binance later put at $6.34 billion. Between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC that day, five hot wallets sent 233.46 million XRP to an attacker address. The FBI has attributed the campaign to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group. Binance has reopened withdrawals in stages and said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion while covering customer losses from its own funds.

The stolen XRP sat quiet at first. Early on Nov. 2, between 02:12 and 04:00 UTC, all about 233 million XRP moved into six new addresses, where it stayed until Nov. 5, Elliptic said. From the afternoon of Nov. 5, two of those six addresses began sending XRP on to dozens of new accounts that swapped it, mostly through THORChain into bitcoin.

A small amount of ether also went through THORChain into bitcoin that evening. The main bitcoin and ether holdings from the theft — the largest pools — have still not moved, Elliptic said. That pattern matches what the firm saw after the Bybit hack: turn freezable and mid-liquidity tokens into bitcoin first, then attempt cash-out later through OTC brokers.

The broader laundering trail is already vast. Since the Binance theft, Elliptic has counted more than 20,000 wallets on 16 blockchains, at least 36 services and at least 65,000 transactions — roughly one new wallet every 30 seconds and one transaction every 10 seconds. About $3.11 billion remains in addresses linked to the Binance theft, mostly as ether and bitcoin. A negligible amount has likely been cashed out so far.

Issuers can freeze many stablecoins. They cannot freeze XRP the same way, and they cannot freeze bitcoin. After Tether and Circle locked down large stablecoin tranches on Oct. 29, the attacker prioritized assets that sit outside that kill switch. Swapping XRP into bitcoin continues that logic: bitcoin is harder to claw back once it is fragmented across fresh addresses.

All of the stolen SOL, about $87 million at Elliptic’s prices, has already been swapped, bridged or mixed, with much of it ending as bitcoin. Most early Tron laundering — about $220 million in TRX — has also run, a flow Bitcoin Magazine noted when covering the quiet shutdown of the T3 Financial Crime Unit before the hack. Only small parts of the main ether and NEAR piles have moved.

U.S. bank supervisors have already checked in with lenders after the Binance breach. For bitcoin markets, the nearer question is how much more of the remaining ~192 million XRP — and eventually the parked ether — gets turned into BTC before anyone tries to cash out.

The test now is whether THORChain operators and other bridges keep blocking the published attacker addresses fast enough to slow the XRP-to-bitcoin pipe, or whether the rest of that pile follows the same overnight path the first $50 million already took.

#5134. IREN’s bitcoin mining revenue drops 58% as firm plans full exit by year-end

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

IREN has been shifting its focus to the AI infrastructure business, and its AI cloud revenue rose 182% in the past quarter to $200 million.

IREN (IREN) reported bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining revenue of $28 million in the three months ended Sept. 30, which is a 58% quarter-over-quarter decline from $67 million and an 88% drop from $233 million a year earlier.

According to the company's 10-Q filing, IREN mined 402 BTC during the period, compared with 2,039 BTC in the same quarter last year. Average operating hashrate fell to 8.8 EH/s from 45.3 EH/s year-over-year. IREN held no bitcoin on its balance sheet as of the end of September as it liquidates mined bitcoin daily.

"We continue to operate Bitcoin miners at certain of our data centers while we transition this data center capacity toward AI Cloud," IREN wrote in the filing. "We aim to substantially complete this transition by December 31, 2026, with immaterial Bitcoin mining operations expected to continue through calendar year 2027."

IREN, which launched in 2018, announced in March last year that it will stop expanding its bitcoin mining operations and shift its focus to AI and high-performance computing through AI cloud services and data center businesses. Competing bitcoin miner firms MARA and CleanSpark have also turned to the AI infrastructure business.

Consequently, the company's quarterly revenue rose 66% sequentially to $228 million, driven by AI cloud revenue of around $200 million, which is up 182% from the prior quarter. The company posted a net loss of $263 million, narrower than the $684 million loss in the previous quarter, while adjusted EBITDA rose to $66 million from $19 million.

IREN said it has $20.6 billion in total assets, including $6 billion of data center assets, with assets and revenue underpinned by customer contracts with Nvidia, Microsoft, and 13 other customers.

The firm's Nasdaq-listed shares fell 17% on Thursday to close at $18.03, in line with a broader sell-off across AI and tech stocks.

Meanwhile, analysts at Bernstein gave IREN an Outperform rating on Friday, raising the price target to $55. They cited the firm’s “self-owned, unencumbered” physical power assets and the flexibility to decide its own pace of development.

#5133. Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Samsung is putting digital dollars in the same app where its users keep their credit cards and boarding passes.

Stablecoin accounts went live in Samsung Wallet on Thursday for eligible U.S. Galaxy users, letting them buy, hold, send, and receive Circle’s USDC on the Solana blockchain without downloading a separate crypto app or managing private keys. The feature reaches about 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the U.S., according to the announcement.

“For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works,” said Lily Liu, president of the Solana Foundation. “What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream.”

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, when the electronics giant tapped Solana for USDC transfers. Samsung Wallet first teased plans to support the stablecoin, which is pegged to the U.S. dollar, over the summer.

The product is built with Bastion, a licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider, and Coinbase, which safeguards the USDC as Bastion’s sub-custodian through its Coinbase Prime Vault. Samsung’s disclosures stress that the account is a crypto wallet, not a bank account.

Users can send USDC to other Samsung Wallet users in the U.S. and to compatible outside crypto wallets with no Samsung transfer fee, though other providers may charge one. They can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees that vary by destination.

Topping up with a credit card, including through Samsung Pay, carries a 4% fee, and withdrawals to a bank account or debit card also come with a fee. New users who make a first top-up of $50 or more can receive $25 in USDC. The service requires Android 13 or later and is open to U.S. residents 18 and older, except those in New York.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools,” said Woncheol Chai, who leads the digital wallet team in Samsung’s mobile business. “Solana helps us bring that experience to Samsung's scale."

Samsung said it expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments and to expand to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

#5131. Rule to Allow Crypto in 401(k)s Arrives at White House for Final Review

A final DOL rule that would ease alternative assets such as private equity, real estate and cryptocurrency into 401(k) retirement plans arrived at the White House on Thursday for review, Bloomberg Law reported, a procedural step that usually precedes public release.

The Office of Information and Regulatory Affairs logged the package on Nov. 5 under the title “Fiduciary Duties In Selecting Designated Investment Alternatives,” RIN 1210-AC38. The filing lists the DOL’s Employee Benefits Security Administration as the authoring office, marks the stage as a final rule, flags it as economically significant and notes that there is no legal deadline for completion.

The DOL unveiled the proposal on March 30. In that draft, plan fiduciaries who follow a documented process when adding private assets to a 401(k) menu would get a legal safe harbor under the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974.

The six factors named in the proposal are performance, fees, liquidity, valuation, performance benchmarks and complexity. Satisfying them does not force plans to offer crypto. It lowers the litigation risk for employers that decide they want to.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 The White House is now reviewing a new rule which will allow Americans to invest in crypto through their 401(k) retirement plans 👀 pic.twitter.com/uXGGIXEzdC

President Donald Trump’s Aug. 7, 2025, executive order, “Democratizing Access to Alternative Assets for 401(k) Investors,” directed the Labor secretary to reexamine ERISA guidance and, where appropriate, propose rules with “appropriately calibrated safe harbors.” The order defined alternative assets to include private-market equity and debt, real estate, holdings in actively managed vehicles that invest in digital assets, commodities, infrastructure and lifetime-income strategies.

The March proposal implements that order. A DOL fact sheet said prudence under ERISA is process-based and that fiduciaries retain discretion to select any designated investment alternative if they follow the outlined review. Deputy Labor Secretary Keith Sonderling cast the approach as neutral: “The department’s days of picking winners and losers are over.”

That stance reverses a prior chill. In May 2025 the DOL rescinded its March 2022 compliance release that told fiduciaries to use “extreme care” before putting cryptocurrency on a 401(k) menu. The department said that phrase was not found in ERISA and restored a context-specific standard.

Later, it was reported that Sens. Bernie Sanders and Elizabeth Warren pressed the DOL to abandon the crypto-friendly path. House Republicans have separately pushed to codify Trump’s crypto order in statute.

OIRA review under Executive Order 12866 is the last interagency checkpoint before a final rule can be published in the Federal Register. Bloomberg Law said the arrival signals the package is nearing completion. The same office cleared the proposed version in late March before the DOL published it; that earlier pass took roughly two months after the January submission, per prior Bloomberg coverage of the roughly $12 trillion 401(k) market.

What is not yet public is the final text itself. Whether the six-factor safe harbor survived comment intact, how digital-asset vehicles are described, and what effective date the DOL chose will only be clear when OIRA returns the rule and the department posts it. Comment volume on the proposal was heavy; industry and labor groups have argued opposite sides of the prudence standard for months.

For bitcoin, the stakes sit in plan menus rather than spot ETF flows. Trump’s August order helped frame digital assets as a retirement-access issue for more than 90 million defined-contribution savers. A finalized DOL safe harbor would not put bitcoin and crypto on every lineup overnight. It would give plan committees a clearer map if they want exposure through an asset-allocation sleeve that holds digital assets.

The test now is how fast OIRA clears the economically significant package — and whether the published final rule still gives fiduciaries the process cover the March draft promised, or whether the comment fight rewrote the safe harbor before the White House stamps it.

#5129. Zcash’s major network upgrade NU7 goes live on mainnet, cutting block time by threefold

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NU7 also activates the Network Sustainability Mechanism, which removes 60% of each block's transaction fees from circulation and stores them in a public reserve.

Zcash Zcash (ZEC) announced that its major network upgrade, NU7, activated on the mainnet at block height 3,543,405 on Nov. 5.

The upgrade reduces the target block interval from 75 seconds to 25 seconds. Block subsidies are scaled down by the same factor so that the rate of new ZEC issuance remains essentially unchanged. The number of blocks between halvings is tripled, which preserves the existing halving schedule and the 21 million ZEC supply cap.

NU7 also activates the Network Sustainability Mechanism. The mechanism removes 60% of each block's transaction fees from circulation and stores it in a public reserve, with miners receiving the remaining 40%. The reserved fees are scheduled for gradual reissuance through block rewards beginning at block 8,939,664, expected to start in 2031.

"Like Bitcoin, Zcash faces a long-term challenge: how to sustain network security as block rewards decline. The NSM helps address that while preserving the 21 million coin cap," said Shielded Labs, an independent organization supporting the development of Zcash. The organization said NSM is its first major mainnet deployment on Zcash.

The upgrade also disallows version 4 transactions. As the original Sprout shielded pool relies exclusively on that format, any ZEC remaining in Sprout becomes unspendable. Approximately 12,224 ZEC sat in the pool at the activation height.

Node operators were required to upgrade software before the activation height to remain on the consensus chain. The changes do not require significant updates for most wallets.

ZEC dropped 5.17% in the past 24 hours to trade at $1,254.7 as of 2:05 a.m. on Friday, amid a broader downturn in the crypto market. The GMCI 30 index, measuring the performance of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, is down 6.9%.

#5128. Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🗳️ Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Market Structure Bill Resurfaces

Bitcoin spent Tuesday night under $76,000, a seven-week low, with traders bracing for whatever a Democratic Congress meant for crypto. By Wednesday afternoon it was back above $80,800, up 4% on the day.

The easy story is that uncertainty lifted and crypto exhaled. And that could be part of it. But oil helped. WTI fell 9% to $88 a barrel, down almost 17% on the week, as Iran’s parliament speaker said voters had “said NO TO WAR.” The DOW jumped 3% on the day and Nasdaq +1.6% in what was a huge green day across markets.

James Butterfill of CoinShares put it plainly: “For Bitcoin, real yields, dollar liquidity and ETP flows still carry more weight than the elections.”

But the policy news that landed Wednesday afternoon matters more than the candles.

Eleanor Terrett reported that House Democrats are discussing expanding Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, originally an amendment to the CLARITY Act in 2025, into a full market structure bill next Congress. The draft would fold in Democratic CLARITY provisions that the House rejected last year, plus provisions Senate Democrats and Republicans had already agreed on before the September cloture vote that killed the bill. Three industry sources described the talks to her.

That’s a genuine surprise. Twenty-four hours ago the consensus was that market structure legislation was dead for two years. Waters is the likely incoming chair of House Financial Services, and she is apparently planning to write a bill rather than hold hearings. But the bill will have some changes.

The new proposals under discussion would give the SEC a larger role supervising crypto markets and leave the CFTC with a smaller one. The draft is expected to carry ethics provisions restricting the crypto activities of Trump and other federal officials. Those provisions are what Democrats wanted all along, and what many thought would kill any chances the bill has of getting signed.

So one of the first courses of action from this Democrat-led Congress is to enact some reasonable changes to the Clarity Act to try to get it passed in 2027. That feels like a very bullish first step.

#5125. Kalshi hit with injunction over sports contracts on California tribal lands

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians.

A federal judge has granted a preliminary injunction barring Kalshi from offering sports-event contracts on the lands of two California tribes.

In a Wednesday order, U.S. District Judge Jacqueline Scott Corley ruled that the injunction covers contracts tied to sports including football, basketball, baseball, soccer, tennis, and Ultimate Fighting Championship events.

The tribes — Blue Lake Rancheria and the Chicken Ranch Rancheria of Me-Wuk Indians — must provide boundary maps by Nov. 13, the judge said.

Kalshi is required to report the status of the steps it has taken to comply with the injunction within 21 days, according to the order.

Sports and gaming attorney Daniel Wallach described the order as the first preliminary injunction secured by tribes against Kalshi nationwide, calling it "a major development in tribal gaming litigation."

The tribes filed the lawsuit in July 2025, alleging that Kalshi's sports event contracts constitute unauthorized Class III gaming under the Indian Gaming Regulatory Act (IGRA) and violate the tribes' gaming ordinances.

Kalshi has argued that its sports event contracts are federally regulated swaps under the Commodity Exchange Act (CEA), and that the CEA's grant of exclusive jurisdiction to the Commodity Futures Trading Commission preempts tribal gaming law.

The judge initially denied the tribes' motion for a preliminary injunction in November 2025. The tribes then appealed, and a Ninth Circuit panel partially reversed the decision in September.

"Reversing the district court's denial of a preliminary injunction in part, the panel held that the tribes were likely to succeed in their claims that Kalshi's sports event contracts on tribal lands violated IGRA and the tribes' gaming ordinances," the panel wrote.

The Ninth Circuit panel also held that the CEA's "exclusive" jurisdiction did not extend to Kalshi's sports event contracts.

The Block has reached out to Kalshi for comment.

Kalshi faces mounting legal challenges, with more states taking action against the prediction markets platform.

On Wednesday, Arkansas Attorney General Tim Griffin sued Kalshi for allegedly operating an illegal gambling platform in the state.

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said, adding that such practice is "squarely prohibited" by Arkansas law.

Last week, Utah also filed criminal charges against Kalshi and two company executives, accusing them of illegally promoting online gambling. Utah has also sued Kalshi and two Susquehanna trading firms for allegedly operating an illegal gambling business.

#5123. Strive (ASST) Posts $474.8 Million Common Net Income as Bitcoin Yield Hits 63.2%

Strive reported $474.8 million of net income attributable to common stockholders for the third quarter ended Sept. 30, a print that arrived Friday alongside a company claim that Class A total return had outperformed bitcoin by 103.7% year to date through the same date.

GAAP net income was $509.0 million, or $5.24 a share on a fully diluted basis. Almost all of that came from a $533.7 million unrealized gain on digital assets marked to fair value. Operating revenue for the quarter was just $2.7 million, so the earnings line is a treasury mark, not a fee business.

In a post on X that led with the outperformance figure, Strive also highlighted a year-to-date bitcoin yield of 63.2% and a bitcoin dollar gain of $9.00 per assumed diluted share outstanding, both measured through Sept. 30. Chairman and CEO Matthew Cole tied those metrics to the stock’s relative showing against the asset.

“Strive has continued to successfully execute on its treasury management strategy, delivering a strong bitcoin yield year to date in 2026, which I believe is the primary driver of our common equity’s outperformance relative to Bitcoin so far this year,” Cole said in the earnings release.

Bitcoin yield, as Strive defines it, is the percentage change in bitcoin per share over a period — not a cash yield. On that measure the company posted 18.5% in the third quarter alone and 19.3% from Oct. 1 through Oct. 30, after the SATS preferred debut.

Strive acquired 8,136 bitcoin in the third quarter at an average cost of about $78,885 and 20,373 bitcoin over the nine months ended Sept. 30. Another 15,124 coins followed between Oct. 1 and Oct. 30. Holdings stood at 28,000 bitcoin on Sept. 30 and 43,124 by Oct. 30, keeping Strive among the largest public corporate stacks after recent multi-thousand-coin buys and smaller weekly adds that still trailed Strategy.

Digital assets at fair value were $2.34 billion on the Sept. 30 balance sheet. Cash had risen to $490.4 million by Oct. 30, and Strive still held Strategy’s variable-rate STRC preferred with a fair value of about $50.1 million.

The capital stack that funded the buys is mostly perpetual preferreds. On Oct. 20 Strive closed a registered offering of 7 million shares of its 15% Series A perpetual preferred (SATS) for about $609 million in net proceeds. Preferred dividends of $34.2 million in the quarter are what turned the $509.0 million GAAP profit into the $474.8 million common figure.

Strip the story to nine-month GAAP and the picture flips. For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Strive posted a net loss attributable to common stockholders of $88.4 million. The third-quarter profit is real under fair-value accounting, but it is concentrated in one mark-to-market line.

ASST itself has not moved in a straight line with the company’s bitcoin-yield scoreboard. Shares closed at $20.59 on Thursday, down about 10% on the day, and traded near $20.70 early Friday. That leaves the stock roughly 29% below its level a year earlier, when Yahoo Finance data put the close near $29, and about 33% under its Oct. 21 local high of $30.75.

The 52-week range on Yahoo Finance runs from $7.02 to $34.40. Year to date, the price path from the Jan. 2 close near $17.30 into the mid-$20s is a gain of roughly 20%, with a February low near the 52-week floor and a September push back toward $30. Through Sept. 30 — the cutoff on Strive’s outperformance claim — ASST had risen about 70% from that January close while bitcoin was down roughly 7% over the same window on Yahoo Finance data, a wide gap even before the company’s own total-return methodology.

The longer chart still carries the scar of 2025, when Asset Entities became the public shell for Strive’s bitcoin-treasury reverse merger and the share price spiked into triple digits on weekly closes before collapsing. Investors who held through that conversion saw a different ride than the tidy year-to-date relative-return figure Strive put at the top of Friday’s note.

Cole said the recent SATS issuance leaves Strive “never been in a better position to generate long-term returns in excess of Bitcoin for our common equity shareholders.” The test for ASST from here is whether another quarter of bitcoin yield and preferred-funded buys can reopen the gap to bitcoin after a year in which the stock still sits well below where it traded twelve months ago, even as the treasury has climbed past 30,000 then 40,000 bitcoin.

#5122. Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman has hit back at criticism of his comments about computer memory, saying they were taken out of context and that he has long argued for the world to make more of it.

"I actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context," Altman tweeted early Wednesday. "I have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time."

but only because...i actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context.

i have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time.

The post came as Altman leaned into the backlash. Replying to users joking about his remarks, he said he "lol'd" at one meme and, when another asked him to rename his X account "Ram Altman," replied "ok yeah," briefly changing his display name before explaining he had only done so because he wanted people to have plenty of memory.

Altman's original comments came in an interview with YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, where he discussed shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom. "From like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time," he said. "You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment."

In the same interview, Altman also blamed memory makers for being slow to expand production, and said he would be "very sad" if prices for chips and other components did not fall.

The remarks drew widespread criticism, including from Elon Musk, who mocked Altman on Sunday with a meme of the Inglourious Basterds character Hans Landa asking, "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote.

The spat comes amid a global memory shortage driven by demand from AI data centers. SK hynix, one of the world's largest memory makers, warned last week that demand for memory would outstrip supply through 2030.

Contract prices for 16GB DDR5 memory chips rose from $8.70 in October 2025 to $48 in September 2026, an increase of more than 550%, according to Tom's Hardware. On an earnings call this week, Chen Ching-wen, CEO of memory maker Team Group, said there was "no downward price trend whatsoever," and warned that some buyers could face a situation where "even money can't buy goods."

The crunch has already pushed up prices for consumer devices. In April, Meta raised the price of its Quest 3 and Quest 3S headsets by $50 to $100, weeks after Sony increased PlayStation 5 prices.

#5119. Nigeria’s central bank introduces stablecoin licensing rules, bans algorithmic versions

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The rules took effect on Nov. 5 and cover virtual asset activities conducted in Nigeria, directed at Nigerian users, or affecting the country's financial system.

The Central Bank of Nigeria has introduced new licensing requirements for stablecoin issuers and other non-security virtual asset service providers, according to regulations that took effect on Thursday.

According to a circular issued to relevant stakeholders, the regulations establish separate licensing categories for naira-referenced and foreign-currency-referenced stablecoins. Issuers must also secure approval for each token before beginning issuance.

The central bank said the rules cover activities carried out in Nigeria or affecting the country's payments system, financial sector, or foreign exchange market, even if the operator has no physical presence in the country.

Under the framework, foreign-currency stablecoins can only be offered, distributed, exchanged, or otherwise made available to Nigerians as Class 2 tokens issued through a Nigerian-incorporated entity holding a Foreign Currency Issuer Licence.

The CBN also requires issuers to obtain written approval for each token, covering its classification, reference currency, reserve arrangements, name, ticker, and supported blockchain networks, per the circular.

The rules also enforce compliance with anti-money laundering and counter-terrorist financing obligations. Entities must implement customer due diligence, sanctions screening, transaction monitoring, and Travel Rule compliance across all custodial wallets, peer-to-peer services, and remittance channels.

Meanwhile, some tokens are prohibited under the rules, including algorithmic stablecoins. The central bank said the ban covers tokens that rely primarily on market dynamics, rather than eligible reserve assets, to maintain their value.

The framework gives the CBN authority over stablecoins and virtual assets used for payments, transfers, remittances, foreign exchange, and settlement. The Securities and Exchange Commission retains oversight of securities and capital-market activities.

Companies operating across both areas must obtain the relevant approvals from each regulator. The regulations took effect on Nov. 5, 2026, with existing operators given 180 days to apply for the appropriate licence or authorization, subject to transitional requirements.

#5118. Former FBI Agent Pleads Guilty to Stealing $2.47 Million in Crypto From Investigations

A former FBI agent pleaded guilty Thursday to stealing about $2.47 million in cryptocurrency with wallet credentials the bureau had collected in its own cases, the U.S. Attorney’s Office for the Eastern District of Virginia announced.

Patrick Steven Yaroch, 37, of Ashburn, Virginia, entered pleas to wire fraud and money laundering in federal court in Alexandria. U.S. District Judge Patricia Tolliver Giles accepted the plea. He faces up to 20 years in prison and is scheduled for sentencing on March 4, 2027.

Court papers put the illicit haul at $2,474,380.66. Prosecutors say Yaroch made roughly 100 fraudulent transfers using credentials tied to 35 separate FBI investigations, drawing on wallets controlled by investigation subjects, adversarial nation-state actors and at least one wallet the FBI itself had created for an operation.

The Justice Department Office of the Inspector General had flagged the case in August after Yaroch’s arrest on a criminal complaint. That earlier account described a smaller set of transfers — about 10 moves totaling roughly $1 million — and noted that Yaroch self-reported the activity to two department employees on July 29, 2026. The plea figures are larger.

From 2017 through early 2025, Yaroch worked in the FBI Boston Division on a squad focused on an adversarial nation-state. From February 2025 through July 31, 2026, he served in the FBI’s Counterintelligence and Espionage Division at headquarters, with access to systems that stored cryptocurrency credentials gathered in investigations.

Beginning no later than December 2024, Yaroch used that access to move coins out of those wallets and into ones he controlled. After the transfers, he swapped the stolen cryptocurrency into other digital assets and ran further transactions meant to obscure its source, nature and ownership.

Between Nov. 25, 2025, and Jan. 13, 2026, he moved about $93,082 into one of his accounts, then shuffled funds among other accounts to cover debts, including credit cards, car payments and loans, the office said.

FOX 5 DC, citing the same U.S. Attorney’s Office release, noted the Portugal planning that appears in the plea papers. Yaroch asked ChatGPT how to maximize investment returns and where to live in the European Union. After the chatbot suggested buying a vineyard in Portugal, he gave Portuguese lawyers a power of attorney on June 15, 2026, to register him with the Portuguese Tax and Customs Authority, obtain a tax identification number and collect the password to his finance portal. He arranged travel to Portugal for September 2026.

The agreed statement of facts goes beyond the dollar figure. Yaroch’s conduct compromised multiple FBI investigations into criminal groups and nation-state adversaries, the office said, and it compromised FBI sources and methods used in foreign intelligence collection and criminal cases. It also “deprived FBI employees of their ability to carry out their functions to protect public safety and national security.”

First Assistant U.S. Attorney Theophani K. Stamos and Daniel S. Wierzbicki, special agent in charge of the FBI Washington Field Office’s Counterintelligence Division, announced the plea. Assistant U.S. Attorneys Kathleen Robeson and Russell Carlberg are prosecuting, with help from the National Security Cyber Section of the Justice Department’s National Security Division and the DOJ inspector general’s Mid-Atlantic region. The case is 1:26-CR-208 in the Eastern District of Virginia.

The admission lands in a week when federal crypto-crime stories keep stacking up — from a failed bitcoin kidnapping plot that produced its own guilty plea last month to FBI tallies putting U.S. crypto fraud losses above $11 billion in 2025, a figure the White House has used to frame a wider crackdown.

What sentencing will do with the gap between Yaroch’s July self-report and the $2.47 million plea figure is still open. So is how many of the 35 FBI cases can be repaired after an insider treated the bureau’s own wallet credentials as a personal ATM. March 4, 2027, is when Judge Giles gets to answer the first question.

#5116. Nail Discoloration Can Be a Mystery. Dermatologists Are Here to Solve It.

Research is uncovering all sorts of new causes—and solutions. Here’s your ultimate guide to both.

NAIL DISCOLORATION AND yellowing isn’t exactly the easiest thing to talk about—and, turns out, pinpointing the cause of it isn’t any easier. With a fairly wide range of visual cues to consider, board-certified dermatologist Dana Stern, MD, explains that ‘discoloration’ is a descriptive catch-all for a nail that has “some kind of color variation from what is considered the normal nail appearance. It’s associated with many different nail issues.”

That makes it particularly difficult to treat. After all, you’ve got to know what’s behind your issue before you can tackle it appropriately. What we can tell you for sure: Whether you’re currently experiencing some unwanted color in your nails or are just curious about future indicators, Dr. Stern says that nail discoloration is an under-discussed topic.

Meet the experts: Dana Stern, MD, is a board-certified dermatologist in New York City. Jennifer Hanway is a board-certified holistic nutritionist.

Well, no longer. Ahead, we break down the specifics on nail discoloration, how to treat and prevent it, and a recently discovered connection between nail discoloration and GLP-1 use.

The spectrum of colors that fall under the umbrella term of nail discoloration are plentiful. “It could be a wide variety of colors including brown, black, white, green, red, purple, and yellow,” says Dr. Stern. Below are common associations with each discoloration shade.

The answer: it depends. It’s actually quite common to have discoloration but it’s hard to nail down (pun intended) the rate of occurrence because it’s a symptom of underlying conditions and not considered a disease within itself.

Nail changes and discoloration frequently occur due to age, normal changes in growth rate, and yellowing are extremely common in older adults. Fungal and bacterial infections, causing greenish nails make up a large percentage of nail discoloration or abnormalities.

Despite overall prevalence and with no single culprit at times, it’s important to understand that nail discoloration can be an indicator of your overall health. “Your nails can reflect aspects of your nutritional status, but they don’t tell you exactly what you’re missing,” explains Jennifer Hanway, a board-certified holistic nutritionist. Though she says that certain changes in nail color have been associated with specific deficiencies, “none is diagnostic on its own,” making it difficult at times to pinpoint the cause and treat it. Dr. Stern mirrors that sentiment, saying that since there is such a wide variety of scenarios for discoloration, it’s important to see a dermatologist.

Hanway says that in some cases, vitamin and mineral deficiencies can cause discoloration. “Vitamin B12 can sometimes cause nail pigmentation; iron-deficiency anemia can cause pale nail beds, and in some cases, a condition known as koilonychia, which is when the nail becomes centrally concave.” Other deficiencies, such as zinc, can affect overall nail growth and appearance.

Though your first step should be to speak to your doctor, it’s also important to take clear photos of your nails for reference and to keep notes. Make reference to which nails are affected, when the changes started, and whether you recently changed your diet, lost weight, started taking supplements, or have begun a new medication. “Speak to your doctor about whether testing for nutritional deficiencies or other factors may be appropriate,” says Hanway.

Depending on your symptoms, medical history, and diet, Hanway says that you may need full bloodwork to test for ferritin, vitamin B12, or other targeted tests.

Dr. Stern says that you can prevent white patches or keratin granulations by using a base coat when you have your nails done or by avoiding prolonged polish wear. If you’re someone who has their nails done regularly, Dr. Stern recommends seeing an experienced nail tech to avoid any chance of encountering discoloration. “Pseudomonas tend to occur in moist spaces on the nail, classically in a lifted acrylic or other type of enhancement, which is why having them done by a professional nail tech can lessen the chances of this occurring.

If you hurt a fingernail where significant blood accumulates under the nail, your dermatologist can evacuate the blood by drilling a small hole in the nail can help to remove the pigment, while also helping to save the nail from falling off.

In terms of nutrition, Hanway says that adequate protein and overall nutrient intake matter. “Nails are made primarily of keratin, a structural protein, so getting enough dietary protein supports overall nail health.” Protein options include eggs, fish, greek yogurt, and nutrient dense foods such as red meat, shellfish, which provide you with iron and zinc. “Supplements should be targeted rather than taken on a hunch, and should ideally be with your healthcare professional’s guidance,” she says.

A multivitamin may help to fill the nutritional gaps when food intake is limited, but it won’t exactly replace a balanced diet or guarantee any nail changes won’t occur. Hanway cautions against taking biotin supplements, even though they are often marketed for stronger nails. “The evidence of benefit in people without a deficiency is limited,” she says. Higher doses of biotin can also interfere with certain laboratory tests, potentially producing misleading results, so be sure to let your healthcare professional know if you are taking it before having bloodwork.

If you’re one of the millions of American women on a GLP-1 medication, know that a recent study suggests there’s a relationship between nail discoloration and these drugs (although it’s not entirely clear on the connection or causality). The 2026 European Academy of Dermatology and Venereology study reported that nail discoloration occurred in 46 percent of GLP-1 users, compared with 17 percent of adults with obesity or type 2 diabetes who had never taken these medications. To add insult to discoloration, nail detachment was also reported by 39 percent of GLP-1 users, compared with 9 percent of the comparison group. Though these are noticeable differences, it’s safe to conclude that there may be an association, but it does not prove that the medication caused these changes.

Dr. Stern points out that the full study was not released to the public, and did not report that about half of the GLP-1 users had nail problems prior to treatment. “This would mean that this study population had a high rate of nail disorders prior to treatment and therefore these nail issues were unrelated to GLP-1 therapy,” she explains. “When the investigators looked at nail changes that began after starting GLP-1, the previously reported associations disappeared entirely. This is a situation where it does not seem that association is equal to causation, but it would be great to have access to the whole study.” Until then, these findings should be considered preliminarily until fully evaluated through peer review.

Her work has appeared in New York, Allure, Glamour, InStyle, Cosmopolitan, New Beauty, and Marie Claire, where she regularly covers skincare, plastic surgery, cosmetic treatments, and makeup trends.

Her work across both media and film is driven by a sharp eye for storytelling, visual culture, and the evolving relationship between beauty and identity.

He was the launch Beauty Director of Dr. Oz THE GOOD LIFE, and has held additional editorial positions at Fitness, Organic Style, Good Housekeeping, Life & Style Weekly, and Woman’s Day and has written for Self, Shape, Seventeen, Redbook, Cosmopolitan, and many more. #5115. Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Plunge, $428 Million in Crypto Longs Get Wiped Out

Bitcoin erased Wednesday's post-election rebound Thursday as a technology stock selloff spread to cryptocurrencies, triggering hundreds of millions in forced liquidations.

Bitcoin fell to about $76,500, down nearly 5% and back near levels seen before Wednesday's jump above $80,000. Ethereum shed more than 5%, while other major tokens suffered comparable losses. Roughly 90% of the top 100 cryptocurrencies were in the red during the selloff, while roughly $428 million in crypto longs were liquidated in one hour.

Wall Street offered no shelter. The S&P 500 lost about 2.6% in late-morning trading, while the Nasdaq sank nearly 4%, approaching a correction, or a 10% drop from its peak.

The election is part of the answer, but anxiety over AI spending and interest rates is making the damage worse.

Democrats' sweep of Congress initially rattled investors expecting tougher oversight of industries favored by President Donald Trump. Stocks rallied Wednesday as traders bet gridlock would limit major policy changes and falling oil prices would ease inflation. Bitwise Europe analyst André Dragosch also told Decrypt the Democratic sweep was "largely priced in."

Thursday brought a different interpretation. Investors weighed what Democratic control might mean for the data-center boom, particularly in states where voters oppose rising electricity costs and new facilities.

And the new political configuration is already moving things. Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed restrictions on data-center development, although not every measure is binding. UBS estimates potential pauses could threaten 30% of planned U.S. data-center capacity additions in 2028, per Reuters.

"We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout," Swissquote senior analyst Ipek Ozkardeskaya told Reuters.

Chipmakers and AI infrastructure companies took the hit first. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index dropped about 8%, while CoreWeave and Nebius fell by double digits. Tesla also tumbled roughly 9.5%, extending losses after Elon Musk spent heavily backing Republican candidates. Investors were also questioning the returns on massive AI investments.

The S&P 500 had the biggest bearish intraday movement in months, dropping almost 4% in minutes.

"The market's moving from the promise of AI to the price of AI," Ted Parkhill, chief executive of Incline Investment Management, told Reuters.

The political shift also threatens crypto's ambitions in Washington. Democratic control complicates efforts to revive the stalled Clarity Act, a proposed federal rulebook for digital assets. The new Congress will not take office until January. Politics alone cannot explain Bitcoin losing thousands of dollars in an hour.

Wednesday's rally looked dramatic, but much of its buying pressure came from traders closing losing bets against Bitcoin. About $378 million in short positions were liquidated in one hour as Bitcoin pushed above $80,000, according to CoinGlass data.

Today’s reversal flipped the liquidation machinery in the opposite direction. Exchanges automatically close underfunded longs as prices fall, generating more selling and the enormous red candles visible on Bitcoin's hourly chart.

Crypto was already vulnerable before the election. The market had lost roughly $300 billion in value over the previous month, amid concerns about AI spending, rising borrowing costs and the fallout from last week's multibillion-dollar Binance hack.

Traders also expect another Federal Reserve rate increase in December as inflation persists. Friday's October jobs report could alter those bets; higher rates generally hurt demand for speculative investments.

Technically, Bitcoin's still-forming daily candle is threatening a bearish engulfing pattern, potentially swallowing Wednesday's green body and erasing the breakout in a single session.

The hourly chart shows selling through $79,000 to near $76,100. The next test is $75,000, while reclaiming $80,000 could ease the pressure—but Thursday's candle has not closed, so the pattern remains unconfirmed.

#5114. Crypto ETFs see over half a billion dollars in outflows amid post-midterm market slide

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

U.S. spot crypto ETFs saw more than $500 million in combined net outflows on Thursday as crypto and tech markets fell across the board.

U.S. spot crypto exchange-traded funds witnessed more than half a billion dollars in net outflows on Thursday as markets reversed initial gains following the U.S. midterms election results.

Among bitcoin ETFs, Fidelity's FBTC led Thursday's net outflows with $199.9 million, followed by BlackRock's IBIT with $105 million and Grayscale's GBTC with $49.9 million, according to data compiled by The Block. Franklin Templeton's EZBC, VanEck's HODL, and Ark Invest's ARKB also saw net outflows, while the remaining ETFs posted zero flows for the day.

Ethereum funds also witnessed heavy net outflows, led by BlackRock's ETHA and ETHB, with $75.2 billion and $22 billion in redemptions, respectively. Fidelity's FETH and Grayscale's ETHE saw outflows too.

Other major crypto ETFs didn't fare much better, accounting for size. U.S. Solana ETF saw a combined $1.2 million in net outflows for the day, Zcash ETFs $11 million, and Hyperliquid ETFs $3 million, according to SoSoValue data. XRP ETFs were the only outlier, with zero flows for the day.

Major U.S. crypto ETFs have now seen a combined net outflow of more than $1.1 billion so far this week.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) fell 5.6% on Thursday, more than erasing an initial 4% gain on Wednesday following Tuesday's U.S. midterm elections. Ether Ethereum (ETH) was hit harder, falling 7.8% for the day, with the GMCI 30 index, representing a selection of the top 30 cryptocurrencies, dropping 6.4%.

Tech stocks also suffered, with the Nasdaq Composite falling 5.5%, and the S&P 500 dropping 4.4% as investors continue to weigh the impact of potential restrictions on the AI infrastructure buildout following the Democrats' sweep of both the House and Senate.

Kyle Rodda, senior financial market analyst at Capital.com, said the Democratic sweep added to concerns over the AI investment boom, with investors already questioning technology valuations and the pace of infrastructure spending.

"The regulatory and political risks arise at a time when the proverbial AI trade was already on shakier foundations," Rodda said, pointing to disappointing earnings and signs of slowing AI spending.

Axel Rudolph, chief technical analyst at IG, told The Block that bitcoin has been "dragged into the AI rout as investors rapidly unwind risk across technology and crypto markets."

Rudolph said bitcoin's correlation with technology stocks had left it exposed to the selloff, with more than $1 billion in leveraged crypto positions liquidated in 24 hours as the market continued to decline.

Friday's U.S. jobs report could complicate matters further for markets, particularly if the figures give the Federal Reserve more reason to raise rates.

Economists expect around 60,000 to 65,000 new jobs, according to Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com. She warned that a stronger jobs report, particularly one showing higher wage growth, could send both Treasury yields and the dollar higher.

Despite the broader market weakness, André Dragosch, head of research for Europe at Bitwise, expects bitcoin to outperform U.S. equities as the AI trade unwinds.

Dragosch said bitcoin is now more sensitive to the dollar and expectations for monetary policy than to economic growth, and could decouple from stocks if financial conditions improve.

#5111. Thailand SEC Files Criminal Complaint Against Bitget Ahead of Dec. 5 Access Block

Thailand's SEC filed a criminal complaint against Bitget operator BG Limited for unlicensed Thai trading from 2022 to 2025.

Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission filed a criminal complaint Thursday against BG Limited, the operator of cryptocurrency exchange Bitget, alleging it ran an unlicensed digital-asset exchange for Thai users from 2022 to 2025, and notified the government to block the platform from Dec. 5.

The complaint, lodged with the Economic Crime Suppression Division, also names Mr. Kritsana Kritsananuwat as an alleged supporter who promoted Bitget through Facebook and YouTube channels, the SEC said in statement No. 241/2026.

From 2022 through 2025, the regulator found, Bitget offered a trading system in the ordinary course of business, charged fees, ran a Thai-language website that could show values in baht, and solicited Thai users on social channels including “Bitget Thailand” on X, “BGB Thai Holder” on Telegram and “bitgetthailand” on TikTok.

That conduct amounts to operating a digital-asset exchange under Section 3 of the Emergency Decree on Digital Asset Businesses B.E. 2561 (2018) without a license, a violation of Section 26 punishable under Section 66, the SEC said. The case now moves to an inquiry official, then prosecutors and the courts.

Separately, the SEC submitted Bitget’s website, other domains and its app to the Ministry of Digital Economy and Society under a 2025 royal decree that authorizes access blocks on unlicensed trading platforms. From Dec. 5, the public “will be unable to access the platform,” the statement said, and investors still holding assets there were told to consider acting before the cutoff.

The enforcement push lands weeks after Bitget announced a planned acquisition of GMO-Z.com Cryptonomics (Thailand), a Bangkok digital-asset exchange and brokerage that already holds Thai licenses, through its interest in BTGT Management (Thailand) Ltd.

Under that deal, GMO Internet and its Thai holding company would transfer their entire stake in GMO-Z — about 49% and 50.94%, respectively — to BTGT or a designee at completion, a Sept. 24 GMO Internet filing showed. Bitget said completion remains subject to regulatory approval and, after a change-of-control nod, it intends to introduce its trading platform to Thai users under local rules.

“Thailand is an important part of Southeast Asia’s digital asset landscape, and this acquisition gives us a great opportunity to establish local operations,” Bitget CEO Gracy Chen said in the Oct. 20 release.

The timetable has already slipped. GMO Internet Group pushed the effective date of the share transfer from Oct. 31 to Nov. 30, citing careful work on Thai regulatory applications and closing procedures. That revised date sits five days before the planned Bitget access block.

CoinDesk reported that Bitget’s own terms of use, updated in October, already list Thailand among jurisdictions where its services are prohibited, and that the SEC has not said whether the criminal complaint will weigh on the GMO-Z change of control.

Thailand has used the same access-blocking tool before. In mid-2025 the SEC referred Bybit, OKX and three other unlicensed platforms for a June 28 cutoff, telling users to move assets first. The Bitget action follows that template — and adds a criminal complaint against the exchange operator and a named promoter.

Licensed venues still sit at the center of Bangkok’s policy mix. Thailand has approved a multi-year capital-gains tax break only for trades on SEC-regulated platforms, and has been expanding regulated crypto products in derivatives and capital markets. Unlicensed platforms get the opposite treatment: investor alerts, access blocks and, now for Bitget, a police referral.

What is not yet clear is whether a criminal case against BG Limited for the 2022–2025 period bars, slows or leaves untouched BTGT’s bid to take over a licensed local operator. The SEC statement does not mention GMO-Z. Bitget has not publicly answered the complaint in a statement posted with the acquisition release.

For Thai users still on Bitget, the practical clock is Dec. 5. For Bitget, the test is whether Nov. 30 closes a regulated door into Thailand — or whether the complaint filed Thursday makes that licensed path the next question the ECD and the SEC have to answer.

#5110. Hypercall to launch first Anthropic-linked pre-IPO options market

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Trading is expected to begin Monday, with calls, puts and more complex strategies available in select non-US jurisdictions.

Hypercall is launching what it says is the first options market tied to Anthropic ahead of its IPO.

The onchain options platform expects trading to begin Monday, founder Jake Sylvestre and growth and marketing head Dan Edlebeck told The Block. The contracts will trade around the clock but will not be available to U.S. customers.

The options are synthetic, cash-settled derivatives. This means that traders will not own actual Anthropic shares or receive any voting rights when a contract expires. Payouts will be calculated on a contract's strike price and reference price at expiry.

The launch would add options to a market that already offers Anthropic-linked perpetual futures contracts. Sylvestre said Hypercall supports everything from basic calls and puts, which he called "vanillas," to more advanced spreads and strategies. "It's a fully featured options platform," he said. "Any kind of option you want to structure, we can do."

Hypercall is built on Hyperliquid, where existing Anthropic-linked perps give market makers a way to hedge the risk of selling options before the company's shares begin trading publicly. Edleback said that gives Hypercall a head start: "We're able to provide options on these assets before anyone else can," he said.

The platform already offers options tied to SpaceX and the S&P 500 as well as major cryptos like bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) and ether Ethereum (ETH), according to the executives. Sylvestre previously reported that Hypercall handled $536 million in options notional trading volume for September.

The rollout comes as traders become less confident that Anthropic will even go public this month or even before the end of 2026.

On Polymarket, the chances of a November IPO fell from roughly 88% Tuesday to as low as 51% by Thursday, while the probability of a January 2027 debut jumped from less than 1% to nearly 17%. The market had generated around $566,000 in trading volume.

A similar market on Kalshi, with nearly $4.9 million in volume, put the chances of an IPO before Dec. 1 at 60%, down from around 88%. Contracts on a debut before Jan. 1 and Feb. 1 traded at 63% and 85%, respectively.

Technology shares also sold off sharply on Thursday, with the Nasdaq falling around 6% during the session amid growing concerns around AI infrastructure spending.

Sylvestre said the market backdrop had changed his concerns about pricing the options, from a post-IPO rally to a potential slump.

"People that market make options are really pricing volatility," he said. "How fast it drops matters as much as how much it drops."

An option can expire worthless, but a price swing won't automatically trigger a margin liquidation like with trading perps on leverage.

Anthropic filed its S-1 on Oct. 26, with plans to list on the Nasdaq under the ticker ANT. The Claude developer reported $16.3 billion in first-half revenue and $9 billion in compute spending. Its prospectus revealed that its five largest customers accounted for around 25% of revenue through the end of September.

#5107. Democrats to Push Broader Crypto Market Structure Bill After Sweeping US Midterms: Report

Democrats to Push Broader Crypto Market Structure Bill After Sweeping US Midterms: Report

House Democrats are reportedly set to progress crypto legislation after sweeping the U.S. midterms.

There are plans to expand the Crypto Investor Consumer Bill of Rights—a Clarity Act amendment led by Dem. Maxine Waters—into a broader market structure bill, journalist Eleanor Terrett tweeted. The proposal would include giving the SEC more power to supervise the crypto markets and limiting the role of the CFTC, sources told Terrett.

🚨SCOOP: House Democrats are discussing plans to expand the Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, @RepMaxineWaters’ amendment to the Clarity Act in 2025, into a broader crypto “market structure” bill next Congress.

The proposal would fold in Democratic Clarity Act…

— Eleanor Terrett (@EleanorTerrett) November 4, 2026

On the surface, the midterm election results could be seen as a major hit to crypto policy in the U.S., with President Trump's Republican administration widely regarded as the pro-crypto party, while the Democrats were deemed more hostile. However, experts, told Decrypt, the tide might be turning and crypto policy evolving into a more bipartisan issue.

Advocacy organization Stand With Crypto endorsed 45 candidates in the midterms, per its election tracker, with 27 pro-crypto candidate wins, 12 losses, and six races still live at the time of writing. Nearly half of the endorsed candidates were Democrats.

"We’re proud to see a majority of our endorsed candidates win their elections," Mason Lynaugh, executive director of Stand With Crypto, said in a statement shared with Decrypt. "To the candidates who won’t return to Washington, thank you for going to bat for crypto-owning Americans when it counted."

Congratulations to the pro-crypto champions who won their elections, including more than 50 pro-crypto new members heading to Congress.

In the closest races, our advocates showed up: in Florida's 9th District, Darren Soto won by 1,477 votes. In that same district, we mobilized… pic.twitter.com/lcq055GNET

— Stand With Crypto🛡️ (@standwithcrypto) November 4, 2026

James Butterfill, head of research at CoinShares, described Republican Bryan Steil as a "meaningful loss" for crypto. The Wisconsin Congressman was the chair of the House digital assets subcommittee and closely involved in efforts to advance the Clarity Act—which were stymied when it stalled in the Senate earlier this year.

That said, Butterfill also highlighted several winners—Democrats Chris Pappas in New Hampshire and James Talarico in Texas, as well as Wyoming Republican Harriet Hageman—who have supported crypto policy. He stressed, however, that the challenge is now "converting" that support into passing legislation. Some believe that could slow an already difficult path for the Clarity Act.

"I wouldn’t say it’s game over for Clarity, but passing it has become tougher," Carlos Guzman, vice president of research at crypto trading firm GSR, told Decrypt. "Some Democrats support clearer crypto rules, but I wouldn’t expect it to be high on their agenda. Any agreement will likely require stronger provisions on things like consumer protection and conflicts of interest."

The Clarity Act looks to establish rules for crypto markets and has been batted back and forth since 2025. Democrats have pushed back on the bill, citing Trump's crypto conflicts of interest, with Senator Elizabeth Warren (D-MA) pushing for the bill to ban officials and their families from profiting from the sector.

"For other crypto policies, the election doesn’t automatically replace the leadership of the SEC or CFTC or stop their rulemaking," Guzman said. "Congressional oversight does change, however, so we might see more scrutiny of regulatory decisions, with Democrats also able to influence agency funding and future appointments."

Butterfill added that he believes crypto is becoming less dependent on the Clarity Act, as the SEC and CFTC push for regulatory clarity without the need for Congress. He also said that AI regulation may take eyes away from crypto, slowing crypto policy in the process.

Following the midterms, Bitcoin has exhibited volatility, and is currently trading at around $76,820, down 4.1% on the day, per Decrypt data.

"The result alone would not make me change my medium-term Bitcoin outlook," Butterfill told Decrypt. "Slower reform could delay some institutional activity, but we would expect the more direct impact to fall on US crypto businesses reliant on new regulatory permissions. We would revisit the outlook if political uncertainty materially weakened investment flows or tightened financial conditions."

#5105. Bitcoin Magazine Books Opens 333-Copy Genesis Book Collector Edition for 3rd Anniversary

Bitcoin Magazine Books opened a third-anniversary campaign Friday for Aaron van Wirdum’s The Genesis Book, offering a redesigned Collectors Edition limited to 333 hand-signed, individually numbered hardcovers in an exclusive Genesis Block slipcase.

Each Collectors Edition hardcover is hand-signed and hand-numbered by van Wirdum, the former Bitcoin Magazine editor-in-chief whose book traces the people, projects and ideas that preceded Satoshi Nakamoto’s peer-to-peer cash design. The standard package is priced at $221 and pairs the book with a limited trading card carrying a Certificate of Authenticity, a collector pin, a sticker sheet, the slipcase and a digital package.

Two one-of-one packages sit outside the ordinary number queue. The #1 package is listed at $5,000 and the #21 package at $2,500; each includes the matching numbered copy, a matching one-of-one collector card and the same accessories as the standard collector package.

Standard collector numbers are assigned in purchase order starting at #2 and skipping #21, the campaign FAQ says. Once preorders close, the signed Genesis print run will not be reproduced.

#5103. TeraWulf’s bitcoin mining revenue falls 69% as HPC leasing surges amid Anthropic buildout

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The former bitcoin mining-focused company now generates most of its revenue from high-performance computing, with further data center capacity under construction.

TeraWulf's TeraWulf, Inc. (WULF) bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) mining revenue fell to $13.2 million in the third quarter from $43.4 million a year earlier, a decline of about 69%, while the Nasdaq-listed company reported $63 million in high-performance computing (HPC) lease revenue, up 774% from $7.2 million in the same period last year.

Total revenue came to $76.2 million, an increase of 51% year over year, according to its latest earnings release on Friday, with HPC leasing accounting for 83% of that revenue compared to just 14% a year earlier.

The company reported $709.9 million in net income attributable to shareholders for Q3, including a $728.4 million non-cash gain from changes in the fair value of warrant and derivative liabilities. Its operating loss widened to $88.2 million from $24.7 million a year ago, while adjusted EBITDA fell to negative $12.8 million.

TeraWulf said it had 102 MW of critical IT capacity generating revenue at its Lake Mariner site in New York as of Sept. 30, while construction is underway on another 336 MW. TeraWulf expects the latest data center building at the site to start generating rent in the fourth quarter and another in early 2027.

TeraWulf previously agreed to a 20-year lease with Anthropic for about 401 MW of critical IT capacity at its Justified campus in Kentucky. The agreement covers approximately $19 billion in contracted revenue over its initial term. Earlier this week, the company announced a Google credit backstop for project financing in support of Anthropic's lease and a definitive engineering, procurement, and construction agreement with Fluor Enterprises, covering three data center buildings. The first phases are scheduled for delivery in late 2027.

Meanwhile, at its Muskie campus in Kentucky, TeraWulf's contracted power capacity has increased from 500 MW to 1 GW, pending regulatory approval.

"Lake Mariner is delivering contracted capacity and generating revenue, Justified has the customer, credit support and construction framework to move forward at scale, and Muskie gives us a contracted path to a gigawatt of power," TeraWulf Chairman and CEO Paul Prager said in the earnings release.

Across its portfolio, the company is targeting another 250 MW to 500 MW of contracted critical IT capacity each year. TeraWulf said it had about $2.4 billion in cash, cash equivalents, and restricted cash at the end of the quarter.

TeraWulf shares are up 8.1% at $10.90 in pre-market trading on Friday following the release.

#5102. Solana Labs’ Leap Launches Mobile App for Trading Tokenized Stocks and Crypto

Solana Labs’ Leap Launches Mobile App for Trading Tokenized Stocks and Crypto

Solana Labs is betting the network’s next wave of traders wants more than meme coins.

Leap, a new company under Solana Labs, launched a beta of its mobile trading app Thursday, letting users trade tokenized stocks, ETFs, metals, currencies, and crypto in one place, around the clock and subject to restrictions.

The app is available in early access on iPhone through Apple’s public TestFlight program, with an Android version coming soon. It isn’t available in the U.K. or other restricted regions.

Leap is built by the team behind Dialect, which Solana Labs acquired earlier this year. Dialect developed Solana Actions and Blinks, a protocol that turns blockchain transactions into shareable links, which launched in 2024, as well as the cashtags feature on X.

The company is pitching the app as an alternative to fast-paced meme coin trading, aimed at people who want to make better-informed decisions over the long term. Its research team curates market-moving news and pins each story to the price chart at the moment it broke.

The app tracks profit and loss across every deposit, withdrawal, trade and fee, including each position’s breakeven price. Users enter an amount in dollars, and each trade executes as an onchain swap.

Leap uses Jupiter for trade routing, Helius for infrastructure and Birdeye for market data.

“The Solana blockchain now confirms a trade in about 150 milliseconds thanks to Alpenglow, so the network is now faster than ever,” said Chris Osborn, Leap’s general manager and Dialect’s founder. Alpenglow a consensus overhaul designed to sharply cut Solana’s confirmation times.

“Every app that brings new people onchain makes the whole ecosystem stronger,” said Solana Labs co-founder and CEO Anatoly Yakovenko.

Leap isn’t charging its own fees at launch, though network and routing costs apply. It plans to use future fees to buy and burn SOL, permanently removing tokens from circulation, and to fund a referral program. Early waitlist members, dubbed the Founders Club, would earn 30% of the trading fees generated by users they invite and 5% from those users’ own referrals.

Leap arrives as tokenized stocks gain traction on Solana and beyond. Securitize has brought tokenized Nvidia, Apple, and Amazon shares to Solana. The trend has also met pushback: 56 companies have objected to having their shares traded on OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.
